\textstyle{{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta\hskip 16.38895pt}
\scriptstyle{\delta}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\big)\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega\circ\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})}

commute. In particular, if
{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}
, then
\vartheta(\omega)=\delta
.

Proof.
Any graded homomorphism
\delta\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
has the form (
5.13
) for a
B
-Manin matrix
M
(see p.
4.2
). By taking
w^{i}
in the left upper corners of the diagrams (
2.4
) we obtain the conditions (
5.8
). The commutativity of the diagram (
5.6
) is checked on the generators
w^{i}
in a straightforward way. ∎

We established the bijection between corepresentations
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
and coactions
\delta\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathcal{B}}
for an arbitrary comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=({\mathcal{A}},\Delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
. This generalises the case
{\mathbf{C}}={\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}
of the bijection (
3.45
), so we denote it by the same letter:
\vartheta\colon\omega\leftrightarrow\delta
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
be coactions of
{\mathbb{O}}
on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
and
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})
. Let
M
and
M^{\prime}
be the corresponding multiplicative first order
B
- and
B^{\prime}
-Manin matrices. Then morphisms
f\colon\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}}),\delta\big)\to\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}}),\delta^{\prime}\big)
in
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
are homomorphisms
f=f_{K}
for
(B,B^{\prime})
-Manin matrices
K
over
{\mathbb{K}}
satisfying (
5.11
). Hence
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})
is a subcategory of
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
.

Proof.
By substituting
w^{i}
to the left upper corner of the left diagram (
2.4
) we obtain the condition (
5.11
). ∎

Remark 5.8
.

Non-linear versions of the quantum representations are the comodule algebras
[
Kass
, Def. 3.7.1]
.
By means of the notion of coaction in
({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
one can define a
comodule algebra
over a bialgebra
{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}},\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
as an object of the category
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{R}})
. The strict monoidal embedding
({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)\hookrightarrow({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
,
{\mathfrak{R}}\mapsto{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u]
, induces (not fully) faithful functor
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{R}})\to\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})
, which embeds the comodule algebras over the bialgebra
{\mathfrak{R}}
to the category of coactions of comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
defined by the formula (
5.1
).
On the other hand, the forgetful functor
U\colon{\mathbf{GrAlg}}\to{\mathbf{Alg}}
has a strict monoidal structure
({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
and hence induces functors between comonoids and between coactions. Thus we get the functor
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})\to\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathcal{A}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})\to\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{B}})
, which translates coactions
\delta
of a comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=({\mathcal{A}},\Delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
on an object
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathbf{GrAlg}}
to the comodule algebra
U{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathbf{Alg}}
over the bialgebra
{\mathbb{B}}=(U{\mathcal{A}},U\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},U\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})
, where the coaction is given by the homomorphism
U\delta_{\mathcal{A}}
. In particular, we obtain a forgetful functor from the category of quantum representations of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}
to the category of comodule algebras over
{\mathbb{B}}
, which generalises the classical situation where the linear representations are regarded as actions in
({\mathbf{AlgSet}},\times)
.

5.2
Operations with quantum representations

We have tree operations with vector spaces: direct sum, tensor product and duality. They induce operations with representations (in the case of tensor product and duality we need an additional structure on the represented object). Similarly, the operations with quadratic algebras give operations with quantum representations. We construct these operations in terms of coactions and Manin matrices.

5.2.1.

Direct sum of Manin matrices.

According to the Manins’ ideas
[
Man88
]
the quantum analogue of the direct sum of vector spaces (in the ‘even’ interpretation) is the (even) tensor product of quadratic algebras (see p.
4.1
and p.
4.1
). Therefore, we describe first the tensor product
{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}
for quadratic algebras
{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
.

For arbitrary matrix idempotents
B\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
and
C\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{n}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n})
define the idempotent
D=\mathop{\mathrm{DiS}}\nolimits(B,C)\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m+n}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m+n})
with the only non-zero entries

\displaystyle D^{ij}_{kl}=B^{ij}_{kl},

\displaystyle D^{\bar{a},\bar{b}}_{\bar{c},\bar{d}}=C^{ab}_{cd}

\displaystyle D^{i,\bar{a}}_{i,\bar{a}}=D^{\bar{a},i}_{\bar{a},i}=\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle D^{i,\bar{a}}_{\bar{a},i}=D^{i,\bar{a}}_{\bar{a},i}=-\frac{1}{2},

(5.18)

where
i,j,k,l=1,\ldots,m
,
a,b,c,d=1,\ldots,n
,
\bar{a}=m+a
.
Then
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{D}({\mathbb{K}})
.

Let
B^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}})
and
C^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{n^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n^{\prime}})
be idempotents as well. Denote
D^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{DiS}}\nolimits(B^{\prime},C^{\prime})\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}+n^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}+n^{\prime}})
. Graded operators
f\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})
and
g\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})
give one more graded operator by the composition

{\mathfrak{X}}_{D}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})\xrightarrow{f\otimes g}{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})\xrightarrow{\sigma^{(23)}}\\
{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})\xrightarrow{\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits}{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{D^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}}).

(5.19)

The condition that this operator is an algebra homomorphism can be written in terms of Manin matrices.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
M
and
N
be
m\times m^{\prime}
and
n\times n^{\prime}
matrices over
{\mathfrak{R}}
. Consider their direct sum

\displaystyle L=M\oplus N=\begin{pmatrix}M&0\\
0&N\end{pmatrix}.

(5.20)

This is an
(m+n)\times(m^{\prime}+n^{\prime})
matrix over
{\mathfrak{R}}
with the only non-zero entries
L^{i}_{j}=M^{i}_{j}
,
L^{\bar{a}}_{\bar{b}}=N^{a}_{b}
, where
i,j=1,\ldots,m
,
a,b=1,\ldots,n
. The matrix
L
is a
(D,D^{\prime})
-Manin matrix iff
M
is
(B,B^{\prime})
-Manin matrix,
N
is
(C,C^{\prime})
-Manin matrix and

\displaystyle M^{i}_{j}N^{a}_{b}=N^{a}_{b}M^{i}_{j},

\displaystyle\forall\,i,j=1,\ldots,m,\quad a,b=1,\ldots,n.

(5.21)

In other words, the graded operator (
5.19
) is a graded homomorphisms iff
f
,
g
are algebra homomorphisms and the corresponding Manin matrices entry-wise commute.

Proof.
By writing the condition
DL^{(1)}L^{(2)}(1-D^{\prime})=0
in entries one sees that it is equivalent to the conditions
BM^{(1)}M^{(2)}(1-B^{\prime})=0
,
CN^{(1)}N^{(2)}(1-C^{\prime})=0
and (
5.21
). ∎

5.2.2.

Direct sum of quantum representations.

Consider coactions
\delta\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathcal{B}}
and
\gamma\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}
of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=({\mathcal{A}},\Delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
on the objects
{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathbf{GrAlg}}
. Let
({\mathcal{A}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
be the bialgebra corresponding to the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}
and
\delta_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}
,
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}
be the corresponding coactions of the bialgebra
({\mathcal{A}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})
. The letter one is the bimonoid
({\mathcal{A}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})
in
({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)
, where
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
and
\eta_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
are the multiplication and unity map in the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
, i.e.
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}(a\otimes b)=ab
,
\eta_{\mathcal{A}}(1)=1_{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
.

We define an analogue of direct sum for the coactions
\delta
and
\gamma
as a coaction of
{\mathbb{O}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}
by using the monoidal category
({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
. According to p.
2.4
we need to introduce the structure of a bimonoid on the comonoid
({\mathcal{A}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
, this is a pair
(\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}},\eta^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}})
such that
({\mathcal{A}},\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}},\eta^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})
is a bimonoid in
({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
, where
{\mathcal{A}}
is understood as the algebra
({\mathcal{A}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}},\eta_{\mathcal{A}})\in{\mathbf{Alg}}
. But
({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)
, so due to Prop.
2.2
we obtain

\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)=\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)\big)=\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits\Big(\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)\big)\Big)=\\
\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits\big(\mathop{\mathbf{{\vphantom{M}}{}_{c}Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)\big)=\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{CommAlg}},\otimes).

This implies
({\mathcal{A}},\mu^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}},\eta^{\prime}_{\mathcal{A}})=({\mathcal{A}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}},\eta_{\mathcal{A}})\in{\mathbf{CommAlg}}
, so we can define the direct sum of coactions by the formula (
2.91
) iff the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
is commutative. We need to use in this case the multiplication
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}
in (
2.91
). In the non-commutative case we define the direct sum in an immediate way, but not for every pair
(\delta,\gamma)
.

Consider the linear map

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\delta_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}}{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\sigma^{(23)}}{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits}{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}.

(5.22)

The conditions (
5.12
) and
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}({\mathcal{A}}_{k}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{l})\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{k+l}
imply that the image of
{\mathcal{B}}_{k}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
under the map (
5.22
) lies in
{\mathcal{A}}_{k+l}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}_{k}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
, so this map also satisfies (
5.12
). Hence it gives the graded map
{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ({\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}})
, which we denote by
\delta\dotplus\gamma
.

Definition 5.10
.

We say that the direct sum of the coactions
\delta,\gamma

exists
if
\delta\dotplus\gamma
is an algebra homomorphism. In this case
\delta\dotplus\gamma
is a coaction of
{\mathbb{O}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}
called
direct sum of the coactions
\delta
and
\gamma
.
Suppose
{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
, so we can consider the corresponding corepresentations
\omega=\vartheta^{-1}(\delta)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
and
\nu=\vartheta^{-1}(\gamma)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
.
If the direct sum of
\delta,\gamma
exists, then

\displaystyle\omega\dotplus\nu:=\vartheta^{-1}(\delta\dotplus\gamma)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}

is a corepresentation of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}
on the object
{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}
called
direct product of corepresentations
\omega
and
\nu
. In this case we say that the direct sum of corepresentations
\omega,\nu

exists
.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
and
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})
,
D=\mathop{\mathrm{DiS}}\nolimits(B,C)
. Let
\delta
and
\gamma
be coactions of
{\mathbb{O}}
given by multiplicative first order
B
- and
C
-Manin matrices
M
and
N
respectively.
The direct sum of the coactions
\delta,\gamma
exists iff
M
and
N
entry-wise commute in the sense of (
5.21
). Thus the direct sum of the corepresentations
\omega,\nu
exists iff the corresponding Manin matrices
M
and
N
satisfy this condition. In this case the coaction
\delta\dotplus\gamma\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{D}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{D}({\mathbb{K}})
and corepresentation
\omega\dotplus\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{D}({\mathbb{K}})\big)\to{\mathbb{O}}
are given by the multiplicative first order
D
-Manin matrix (
5.20
).

Proof.
Since
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}={\mathcal{C}}_{0}={\mathbb{K}}
we have
({\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}})_{1}={\mathcal{B}}_{1}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
, so the first order component of
\delta\dotplus\gamma
coincides with
\delta_{1}\oplus\gamma_{1}
. Hence the graded map
\delta\dotplus\gamma\colon{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ({\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}})
is given by the matrix
L=M\oplus N
. Since
M
and
N
are multiplicative first order matrices, so is
L
. The rest follows from Prop.
5.6
and
5.9
. ∎

In general, the operation of direct sum does not give a monoidal structure on
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
nor on
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})
. However if
\delta,\gamma,\delta^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}
are coactions of
{\mathbb{O}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}
,
{\mathcal{C}}
,
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}
such that the direct sums of the coactions
\delta,\gamma
and of
\delta^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}
exist, then due to Prop.
5.11
and
5.7
any morphisms
f\colon({\mathcal{B}},\delta)\to({\mathcal{B}}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})
and
g\colon({\mathcal{C}},\gamma)\to({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime})
in
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
give a morphism

f\dotplus g\colon({\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}},\delta\dotplus\gamma)\to({\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}\dotplus\gamma^{\prime}).

Thus we have the functor from a full subcategory of
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})\times\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
to
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
. The objects of this subcategory are the pairs of coactions for which the direct sum exists. Note that the direct sum of the coactions
\delta,\gamma
exists iff it exists for
\gamma,\delta
; in this case the isomorphism
\sigma_{{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}}\colon{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}\cong{\mathcal{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}
gives the isomorphism
({\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}},\delta\dotplus\gamma)\cong({\mathcal{C}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}},\gamma\dotplus\delta)
in
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
. In particular, if the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
is commutative, then the direct sum functor
({\mathcal{B}},\delta)\dotplus({\mathcal{C}},\gamma)=({\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}},\delta\dotplus\gamma)
defines a symmetric monoidal structure on the category
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
and on its subcategory
\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})
.

5.2.3.

Categorical product of quantum representations.
In p.
4.1
we constructed the coproduct of quadratic algebras, which corresponds to the product of quantum linear spaces in the category
{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Let us modify the definition of direct sum of corepresentations for the case of coproduct
{\mathcal{B}}\amalg{\mathcal{C}}
.

Let
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
and
{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})
be quadratic algebras for idempotents
B\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
and
C\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{n}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n})
. Define the idempotent
E=\mathop{\mathrm{CoP}}\nolimits(B,C)\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m+n}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m+n})
with the only non-zero entries

\displaystyle E^{ij}_{kl}=B^{ij}_{kl},

\displaystyle E^{\bar{a},\bar{b}}_{\bar{c},\bar{d}}=C^{ab}_{cd},

(5.23)

where
i,j,k,l=1,\ldots,m
,
a,b,c,d=1,\ldots,n
,
\bar{a}=m+a
(the entries

E^{i,\bar{a}}_{i,\bar{a}}
,
E^{\bar{a},i}_{\bar{a},i}
,

E^{i,\bar{a}}_{\bar{a},i}
,
E^{i,\bar{a}}_{\bar{a},i}
are also zero in this case).
Then
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\amalg{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{E}({\mathbb{K}})
.

Proposition 5.12
.

Consider idempotents
B^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}})
,
C^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{n^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n^{\prime}})
and
E^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{CoP}}\nolimits(B^{\prime},C^{\prime})\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}+n^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}+n^{\prime}})
.
Let
M
and
N
be
m\times m^{\prime}
and
n\times n^{\prime}
matrices over
{\mathfrak{R}}
. Let
L=M\oplus N
be defined as in Prop.
5.9
. Then
L
is a
(E,E^{\prime})
-Manin matrix iff
M
is
(B,B^{\prime})
-Manin matrix and
N
is
(C,C^{\prime})
-Manin matrix.

Proof.
The same as for Prop.
5.9
. ∎

Note that in this case the commutativity condition (
5.21
) is not needed. Therefore we can define corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}\amalg{\mathcal{C}}
for any corepresentations
\omega,\nu
on
{\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
.

Definition 5.13
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
and
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})
. Then we have
{\mathcal{B}}\amalg{\mathcal{C}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{E}({\mathbb{K}})
for
E=\mathop{\mathrm{CoP}}\nolimits(B,C)
. Consider corepresentations
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
and
\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
given by multiplicative first order
B
- and
C
-Manin matrices
M
and
N
over
{\mathcal{A}}
. By taking into account Prop.
5.12
we see that
L=M\oplus N
is a multiplicative first order
E
-Manin matrix over
{\mathcal{A}}
. We call the corepresentation
\omega\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}\amalg{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
given by the matrix
L

coproduct of corepresentations

\omega
and
\nu
. Let
\delta=\vartheta(\omega)
,
\gamma=\vartheta(\nu)
be coactions of
{\mathbb{O}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}
given by the matrices
M
and
N
. By
coproduct of coactions

\delta
and
\gamma
we mean the coaction
\delta\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\gamma=\vartheta(\omega\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\nu)
on
{\mathcal{B}}\amalg{\mathcal{C}}
given by the matrix
L
.

Note that the coproduct of corepresentations (coactions) is given by the same matrix
L
as the their direct sum, but this is a corepresentation (coaction) on another quadratic algebra (see the warning in p.
5.1
).

By using the results of p.
4.1
and Prop.
5.7
one can check that
({\mathcal{B}}\amalg{\mathcal{C}},\omega\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\nu)
is a coproduct of the objects
({\mathcal{B}},\omega)
and
({\mathcal{C}},\nu)
in the category
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})
. Hence the latter is a category with finite coproducts (its initial object is the algebra
{\mathbb{K}}
with the trivial corepresentation). If we denote this coproduct by
({\mathcal{B}},\omega)\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}({\mathcal{C}},\nu):=({\mathcal{B}}\amalg{\mathcal{C}},\omega\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\nu)
, then we obtain a bifunctor
-\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}-\colon\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})\times\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})\to\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})
. The coproduct of corepresentations corresponds to the categorical product of quantum representations in
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}}
.

5.2.4.

Tensor product of Manin matrices.
The quantum analogue of the tensor product is the Manin product (see p.
4.1
and p.
4.1
). Let us describe the Manin product of the connected finitely generated quadratic algebras
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
and
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})
, where
B\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
and
C\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{n}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n})
are matrix idempotents.

Proposition 5.14
.

The operator
F=\mathop{\mathrm{TeP}}\nolimits(B,C)\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n})
defined as

\displaystyle F=\sigma^{(23)}(B\otimes 1+1\otimes C-B\otimes C)\sigma^{(23)},

(5.24)

is an idempotent.
We have
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{F}({\mathbb{K}})
.

Proof.
The idempotentness of (
5.24
) is equivalent to the idempotentness of the operator

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}=\sigma^{(23)}F\sigma^{(23)}=B\otimes 1+1\otimes C-B\otimes C\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n}),

(5.25)

which, in turn, directly follows from
B^{2}=B
and
C^{2}=C
. Let
W=({\mathbb{K}}^{m})^{*}
and
V=({\mathbb{K}}^{n})^{*}
, then we have
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})=TW/(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits B^{*})
and
{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})=TV/(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits C^{*})
, where
B^{*}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W^{\otimes 2})
and
C^{*}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(V^{\otimes 2})
are transposed idempotents (see p.
4.2
). The algebra
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})
is the quotient
T(W\otimes V\big)/(R_{\mathrm{w}})
, where
R_{\mathrm{w}}=\sigma^{(23)}(\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits B^{*}\otimes V^{\otimes 2}+W^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits C^{*})
. Hence it is enough to check that the subspace
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits B^{*}\otimes V^{\otimes 2}+W^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits C^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(B^{*}\otimes 1)+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(1\otimes C^{*})\subset W^{\otimes 2}\otimes V^{\otimes 2}
coincides with
\sigma^{(23)}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits F^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits\widetilde{F}^{*}
. We have
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits\widetilde{F}^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(B^{*}\otimes 1+1\otimes C^{*}-B^{*}\otimes C^{*})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(B^{*}\otimes 1)+\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(1\otimes C^{*})
. On the other hand, the formulae
B^{*}\otimes 1=\widetilde{F}(B^{*}\otimes 1)
and
1\otimes C^{*}=\widetilde{F}(1\otimes C^{*})
imply
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(B^{*}\otimes 1)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\widetilde{F}^{*})
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(1\otimes C^{*})\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\widetilde{F}^{*})
. ∎

Consider two more idempotents
B^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}})
,
C^{\prime}\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{n^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n^{\prime}})
. Define
F^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{DiS}}\nolimits(B^{\prime},C^{\prime})\in\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}+n^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}+n^{\prime}})
. We have
F^{\prime}=\sigma^{(23)}\widetilde{F}^{\prime}\sigma^{(23)}
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}^{\prime}=B^{\prime}\otimes 1+1\otimes C^{\prime}-B^{\prime}\otimes C^{\prime}.

(5.26)

For graded homomorphisms
f\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})
and
g\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})
consider a graded operator

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{X}}_{F}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})\xrightarrow{f\circ g}{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})\xrightarrow{(\mu_{\mathfrak{R}})}{\mathfrak{X}}_{F^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}}),

(5.27)

where
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})\xrightarrow{(\mu_{\mathfrak{R}})}{\mathfrak{X}}_{D^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})
is the multiplication in the tensor factors
{\mathfrak{R}}
, that is a graded operator with the components

{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})_{k}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})_{k}={\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})_{k}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})_{k}\xrightarrow{\sigma^{(23)}}\\
{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})_{k}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})_{k}\xrightarrow{\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits}{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})_{k}\otimes{\mathfrak{X}}_{C^{\prime}}({\mathbb{K}})_{k}={\mathfrak{X}}_{F^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{R}})_{k}.

The homomorphisms
f
and
g
are given by some
(B,B^{\prime})
- and
(C,C^{\prime})
-Manin matrices
M
and
N
. These are
m\times m^{\prime}
and
n\times n^{\prime}
matrices over
{\mathfrak{R}}
. The graded operator (
5.27
) is given by the matrix
P=M^{(1)}N^{(2)}
, this is an
(mn)\times(m^{\prime}n^{\prime})
matrix over
{\mathfrak{R}}
with the entries

\displaystyle P^{ia}_{jb}=M^{i}_{j}N^{a}_{b}.

(5.28)

In the notations of p.
4.3
this is the operator
P=M\mathbin{\dot{\otimes}}N\colon{\mathbb{K}}^{m^{\prime}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n^{\prime}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{n}
.

Proposition 5.15
.

Suppose
M
and
N
are
(B,B^{\prime})
- and
(C,C^{\prime})
-Manin matrices. The matrix
P=M^{(1)}N^{(2)}=M\mathbin{\dot{\otimes}}N
is an
(F,F^{\prime})
-Manin matrix iff

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}M^{(1)}(M^{(2)}N^{(3)}-N^{(3)}M^{(2)})N^{(4)}(1-\widetilde{F}^{\prime})=0,

(5.29)

where
\widetilde{F}
and
\widetilde{F}^{\prime}
are given by the formulae (
5.25
), (
5.26
).
Let
f=f_{M}
and
g=f_{N}
, then (
5.29
) is the condition for the graded operator (
5.27
) to be an algebra homomorphism.

Proof.
The matrix
P
is an
(F,F^{\prime})
-Manin matrix iff

FM^{(1)}N^{(2)}M^{(3)}N^{(4)}(1-F^{\prime})=0
. By means of the conjugation by
\sigma^{(23)}
we obtain the equivalent condition

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}M^{(1)}N^{(3)}M^{(2)}N^{(4)}(1-\widetilde{F}^{\prime})=0.

(5.30)

Due to
BM^{(1)}M^{(2)}=BM^{(1)}M^{(2)}B^{\prime}
and
CN^{(1)}N^{(2)}=CN^{(1)}N^{(2)}C^{\prime}
we derive

\displaystyle(B\otimes 1)M^{(1)}M^{(2)}N^{(3)}N^{(4)}

\displaystyle=(B\otimes 1)M^{(1)}M^{(2)}N^{(3)}N^{(4)}(B^{\prime}\otimes 1),

\displaystyle\big((1-B)\otimes C\big)M^{(1)}M^{(2)}N^{(3)}N^{(4)}

\displaystyle=\big((1-B)\otimes C\big)M^{(1)}M^{(2)}N^{(3)}N^{(4)}(1\otimes C^{\prime}).

Addition and multiplication by
1-\widetilde{F}^{\prime}=(1-B^{\prime})\otimes(1-C^{\prime})
on the right gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}M^{(1)}M^{(2)}N^{(3)}N^{(4)}(1-\widetilde{F}^{\prime})=0.

(5.31)

By subtracting (
5.30
) from (
5.31
) we get (
5.29
). ∎

Note that the condition (
5.29
) is valid for entry-wise commuting matrices
M
and
N
in the sense of Prop.
5.9
. However, the condition (
5.21
) is not necessary. For example, if
B,B^{\prime},C,C^{\prime}
are
0
, then any matrices
M
and
N
satisfy (
5.29
).

5.2.5.

Tensor product of quantum representations.

Let
{\mathbb{O}}=({\mathcal{A}},\Delta,\varepsilon)
be a comonoid in
({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
, where
{\mathcal{A}}=({\mathcal{A}},\mu_{\mathcal{A}},\eta_{\mathcal{A}})\in{\mathbf{GrAlg}}
. By tensor product of its coactions or corepresentations on
{\mathcal{B}}
and
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
we mean the corresponding coaction or, respectively, corepresentation on
{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
. One can define it as a monoidal product in the category
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})
introduced in p.
2.4
if
{\mathbb{O}}
has additionally a structure of bimonoid
{\mathbb{B}}=({\mathcal{A}},\mu,\eta,\Delta,\varepsilon)
, where
\mu\colon{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
and
\eta\colon{\mathbb{K}}[u]\to{\mathcal{A}}
are morphisms of graded algebras compatible with the comonoid structure of
{\mathbb{O}}
in the sense of p.
2.4
(the homomorphisms
\mu
,
\eta
are not to be confused with the maps
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathcal{A}}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
and
\eta_{\mathcal{A}}\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathcal{A}}
). This means the following conditions on the components
\mu_{k}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{k}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{k}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{k}
and
\eta_{k}\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{k}
. First, they must give an associative multiplication on each component
{\mathcal{A}}_{k}
with the unities
1_{k}=\eta_{k}(1)=\eta(u^{k})\in{\mathcal{A}}_{k}
such that
({\mathcal{A}}_{k},\mu_{k},\eta_{k},\Delta_{k},\varepsilon_{k})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)
, where
\Delta_{k}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{k}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{k}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{k}
and
\varepsilon_{k}\colon{\mathcal{A}}_{k}\to{\mathbb{K}}
are the graded components of
\Delta
and
\varepsilon
. Second, they must be compatible with the multiplication
\mu_{\mathcal{A}}(a\otimes b)=ab
and with the unity
1_{\mathcal{A}}=\eta_{\mathcal{A}}(1)
, that is
\mu_{k+l}(a_{k}b_{l}\otimes a^{\prime}_{k}b^{\prime}_{l})=\mu_{k}(a_{k}\otimes a^{\prime}_{k})\mu_{l}(b_{l}\otimes b^{\prime}_{l})
for any
a_{k},a^{\prime}_{k}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{k}
,
b_{l},b^{\prime}_{l}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{l}
and
1_{k+l}=1_{k}1_{l}
,
1_{0}=1_{\mathcal{A}}
. In particular, the vector space
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
has two multiplications
(\mu_{A})_{0}
and
\mu_{0}
with the same unity. By Eckmann–Hilton Principle (Prop.
2.2
) the compatibility condition
\mu_{0}(ab\otimes cd)=\mu_{0}(a\otimes c)\mu_{0}(d\otimes d)
implies that
\mu_{0}=(\mu_{\mathcal{A}})_{0}
and this is a commutative multiplication on
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
. Moreover, one can show that a monoid structure
(\mu,\eta)
on a graded algebra
{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes S(V)
exists only if
\dim V\leqslant 1
.

Consider a bialgebra
({\mathfrak{R}},\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
. The embedding
{\mathbf{Alg}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}
gives the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\Delta,\varepsilon)
defined by the formula (
5.1
).

If
{\mathfrak{R}}\in{\mathbf{CommAlg}}
, then the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
has a unique bimonoid structure:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\mu,\eta,\Delta,\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\mu=\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]},

\displaystyle\eta=\eta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]}.

(5.32)

In this case we get a tensor product of coactions as the monoidal product in
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}})
.

Here we have a situation similar to one from p.
5.2
: we can define the tensor product without commutativity condition on
\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}
, but this product not always exists.
Namely, consider an arbitrary
{\mathfrak{R}}\in{\mathbf{Alg}}
. Note that
({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u])\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathfrak{R}})
. Let
\delta\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathfrak{R}})
,
\gamma\colon{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})\to{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathfrak{R}})
be coactions of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
and
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})
given by multiplicative
B
- and
C
-Manin matrices
M
and
N
over
{\mathfrak{R}}
. By substituting the homomorphisms
f=f_{M}=\delta
and
g=f_{N}=\gamma
to (
5.27
) we obtain the graded operator

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{X}}_{F}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathbb{K}})\xrightarrow{\delta\circ\gamma}{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathfrak{R}})\circ{\mathfrak{X}}_{C}({\mathfrak{R}})\xrightarrow{(\mu_{\mathfrak{R}})}{\mathfrak{X}}_{F}({\mathfrak{R}}),

(5.33)

where
F=\mathop{\mathrm{TeP}}\nolimits(B,C)=\sigma^{(23)}(B\otimes 1+1\otimes C-B\otimes C)\sigma^{(23)}
. Denote the composition (
5.33
) by
\delta\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\gamma
. Due to Prop.
5.15
this operator is a coaction of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}
iff the matrices
M
and
N
satisfy (
5.29
).

Definition 5.16
.

We say that the tensor product of the coactions
\delta,\gamma

exists
if
\delta\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\gamma
is an algebra homomorphism, i.e. if

\delta\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\gamma\colon{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}={\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}

is a coaction of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}
, we call it
tensor product of the coactions
\delta
and
\gamma
.
Let
\omega=\vartheta^{-1}(\delta)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
,
\nu=\vartheta^{-1}(\gamma)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
be the corresponding corepresentations of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
. If
\delta\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\gamma
is a coaction, then

\omega\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu:=\vartheta^{-1}(\delta\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\gamma)\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}

is a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}
called
tensor product of the corepresentations
\omega
and
\nu
. In this case we say that the tensor product of corepresentations
\omega,\nu

exists
.

It follows from Prop.
5.4
again, that the tensor product of corepresentations is a functor
-\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}-
from a full subcategory of
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})\times\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})
to
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})
. It has the form
({\mathcal{B}},\omega)\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}({\mathcal{C}},\nu)=({\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}},\omega\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu)
. If
{\mathfrak{R}}\in{\mathbf{CommAlg}}
, this functor is defined on the whole
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})\times\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})
and, consequently, gives a structure of monoidal category
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}),\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\big)
. It is symmetric due to commutativity of
\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}
.

Similarly we can define a tensor product of the quantum representations
\omega
and
\nu
on the black Manin product
{\mathcal{B}}\bullet{\mathcal{C}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{G}({\mathbb{K}})
, where
G=\sigma^{(23)}(B\otimes C)\sigma^{(23)}
is the corresponding idempotent. Since
({\mathcal{B}}\bullet{\mathcal{C}})_{1}=({\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}})_{1}
, a corepresentation on the black Manin product can be defined by the same matrix
P=M\dot{\otimes}N=M^{(1)}N^{(2)}
, which is multiplicative with respect to
\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}
. It is a
G
-Manin matrix iff

\displaystyle(B\otimes C)M^{(1)}(M^{(2)}N^{(3)}-N^{(3)}M^{(2)})N^{(4)}(1-B\otimes C)=0.

(5.34)

Under this condition we obtain
black tensor product

\displaystyle\omega\mathbin{\dot{\bullet}}\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}\bullet{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}

(5.35)

defined by the matrix
M\dot{\otimes}N
.
This is a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on the quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{B}}\bullet{\mathcal{C}}
.
We get one more partially defined binary operation with quantum representations. In the case
{\mathfrak{R}}\in{\mathbf{CommAlg}}
, it is a bifunctor
-\mathbin{\dot{\bullet}}-\colon\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})\times\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})\to\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}})
.

5.2.6.

Opposite and coopposite quantum representations.

For an arbitrary comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=({\mathcal{A}},\Delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
denote
{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{op}}=({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}},\Delta,\varepsilon)
, where
{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}}
is the opposite graded algebra (the opposite monoid in
({\mathbf{GrVect}},\otimes)
in the sense of p.
2.4
). The corresponding bialgebra
({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})
is the bimonoid in
({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)
opposite to the bimonoid
({\mathcal{A}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})
. This bialgebra satisfies (
5.2
), so the triple
{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{op}}=({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}},\Delta,\varepsilon)
is also a comonoid in
({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
.

The opposite graded algebra to a quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{A}}=TV/(R)\in{\mathbf{QA}}
has the form
{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}}=TV/(\sigma_{V,V}R)
(see
[
Man88
]
). Let
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
for some
B\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
and denote
B^{(21)}=\sigma\cdot B\cdot\sigma\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
, where
\sigma=\sigma_{{\mathbb{K}}^{m},{\mathbb{K}}^{m}}
. This is an
m^{2}\times m^{2}
matrix over
{\mathbb{K}}
with entries
(B^{(21)})^{ij}_{kl}=B^{ji}_{lk}
. The corresponding quadratic algebra is
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{B^{(21)}}({\mathbb{K}})
. Let
{\mathcal{M}}=({\mathcal{M}}^{i}_{j})
be the universal
B
-Manin matrix. The algebra
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}})
is generated by the same
{\mathcal{M}}^{i}_{j}
, but with the relations
B^{(21)}{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}(1-B^{(21)})=0
. In other words, the matrix
{\mathcal{M}}
considered as a matrix over
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}},{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}})
is the universal
B^{(21)}
-Manin matrix. On the other hand the algebra
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{B}})^{\mathrm{op}}
is generated by
{\mathcal{M}}^{i}_{j}
with the relations
B{\mathcal{M}}^{(2)}{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}(1-B)=0
, which is equivalent to the previous relations. Thus we obtain
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}})=\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})^{\mathrm{op}}
for any
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
.

Let
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
be a corepresentation. The same linear map is a comonoid morphism
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}})=\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{op}}
, so this is a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{op}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Let us denote it by
\omega^{\mathrm{op}}
and call
the corepresentation opposite to
\omega
.
Note that the corepresentations
\omega
and
\omega^{\mathrm{op}}
is given by the same multiplicative
B
-Manin matrix
M
, which is considered as
B
- or as
B^{(21)}
-Manin matrix over
{\mathcal{A}}
or over
{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}}
respectively.

We have the algebra isomorphism
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{B}})={\mathcal{B}}^{!}\bullet{\mathcal{B}}=({\mathcal{B}}^{!})^{!}\bullet{\mathcal{B}}^{!}=\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{!},{\mathcal{B}}^{!})
, so the formulae (
4.90
) implies the comonoid isomorphism
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{!})=\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})^{\mathrm{cop}}
(see
[
Man88
, § 5.10]
).
Any corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}
can be regarded as a corepresentation
\omega^{\mathrm{cop}}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{!})\to{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{cop}}
of the coopposite comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}^{\mathrm{cop}}
on the Koszul dual quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{B}}^{!}=\Xi_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
, we call it
coopposite corepresentation
to the corepresentation
\omega
. It corresponds to the
(1-B^{\top})
-Manin matrix
M^{\top}
over
{\mathcal{A}}
, which is multiplicative with respect to
\Delta^{\mathrm{cop}}
.

By applying these two operations (in any order) to the corepresentation
\omega
we obtain opposite coopposite corepresentation
\omega^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big(({\mathcal{B}}^{!})^{\mathrm{op}}\big)\to{\mathbb{O}}^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}
. This is a corepresentation of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}=({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}},\Delta^{\mathrm{cop}},\varepsilon)
, which corresponds to the opposite coopposite bialgebra
({\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{op}},\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathrm{cop}},\varepsilon_{\mathcal{A}})
, on the quadratic algebra
({\mathcal{B}}^{!})^{\mathrm{op}}=({\mathcal{B}}^{\mathrm{op}})^{!}=\Xi_{B^{(21)}}({\mathbb{K}})
.

5.2.7.

Dual and Koszul dual quantum representations.

Assume that the bialgebra
{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}},\mu_{\mathfrak{R}},\eta_{\mathfrak{R}},\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}})
is a Hopf algebra and let
\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}
be its antipode. This is an algebra anti-homomorphism as well as a coalgebra anti-homomorphism in the sense

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}\cdot\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}=\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{op}}\cdot(\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}),

\displaystyle\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}\cdot\eta_{\mathfrak{R}}=\eta_{\mathfrak{R}},

(5.36)

\displaystyle(\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}})\cdot\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}=\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{cop}}\cdot\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}\cdot\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}=\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}

(5.37)

(see
[
Kass
, Th. III.3.4]
). This means that
\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}
is a morphism
{\mathfrak{R}}^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}
in
\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)
, where
{\mathfrak{R}}^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}=({\mathfrak{R}},\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{op}},\eta_{\mathfrak{R}},\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{cop}},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}})
. By tensoring with
{\mathbb{K}}[u]
we obtain the morphism
\zeta=\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]}\colon{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
, where
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\Delta=\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]},\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]})
is the bialgebra
{\mathfrak{R}}
embedded to
\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
and
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}=({\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\Delta^{\mathrm{cop}},\varepsilon)
.

Let
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
be a corepresentation of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on a quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
given by a multiplicative
B
-Manin matrix
M=(M^{i}_{j})
over
{\mathfrak{R}}
. Then the composition

\displaystyle\omega^{D}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big(({\mathcal{B}}^{!})^{\mathrm{op}}\big)\xrightarrow{\omega^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}}{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{{\mathrm{op}},{\mathrm{cop}}}\xrightarrow{\zeta}{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}

(5.38)

is a corepresentation of the same comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on the opposite Koszul dual quadratic algebra
({\mathcal{B}}^{!})^{\mathrm{op}}=\Xi_{B^{(21)}}({\mathbb{K}})
. Let us call it
dual corepresentation
to the corepresentation
\omega
. This is a quantum analogue of dual (contragredient) representation on a dual vector space.

Since
M
is a multiplicative matrix over a Hopf algebra, it is invertible. The entries of the inverse matrix
M^{-1}
are
(M^{-1})^{i}_{j}=\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}(M^{i}_{j})
. Due to the fact that
\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}
is an anti-endomorphism of
{\mathfrak{R}}
the matrix
M^{-1}
is a
B^{(21)}
-Manin matrix. The dual corepresentation (
5.38
) is given by the
(1-B^{(21)})^{\top}
-Manin matrix
(M^{\top})^{-1}=(M^{-1})^{\top}
over
{\mathfrak{R}}
, which is multiplicative with respect to the comultiplication
\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}
.

Consider the corepresentation opposite to (
5.38
). It has the form

\displaystyle\omega^{KD}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}}^{!})\xrightarrow{\omega^{{\mathrm{cop}}}}{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{{\mathrm{cop}}}\xrightarrow{\zeta}{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{{\mathrm{op}}}.

(5.39)

This is a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{op}}
on the Koszul dual quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{B}}^{!}
. Let us call it
Koszul dual corepresentation
to the corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
. It corresponds to the
(1-B^{\top})
-Manin matrix
(M^{\top})^{-1}=(M^{-1})^{\top}
over
{\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Let
\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
be a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on
{\mathcal{C}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
and
N
be the corresponding multiplicative matrix. By using (
4.17
) we get
{\mathcal{B}}\bullet{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{B}}^{!}\circ{\mathcal{C}}^{!})^{!}
. One can show that the tensor product of
\omega^{KD},\nu^{KD}
exists iff the black tensor product of
\omega,\nu
exists (the condition (
5.34
) fulfils). In this case the Koszul dual to
\omega^{KD}\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu^{KD}
is the corepresentation
\big(\omega^{KD}\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu^{KD}\big)^{KD}
of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on the black Manin product
{\mathcal{B}}\bullet{\mathcal{C}}
, given by the multiplicative matrix
M^{(1)}N^{(2)}
. It coincides with the corepresentation (
5.35
).

Suppose the Hopf algebra
{\mathfrak{R}}
is commutative as an algebra. Then the Koszul dual to a corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
is a corepresentation
\omega^{KD}
of the same comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}^{\mathrm{op}}={\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on the object
{\mathcal{B}}^{!}
. The comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
can be considered as a commutative bimonoid
{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\mu=\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]},\eta=\eta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]},\Delta,\varepsilon)
, it is a Hopf monoid with the antipode
\zeta
.
By virtue of the formulae (
4.17
), (
4.81
) we derive
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}})={\mathcal{B}}^{!}\bullet{\mathcal{C}}=({\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}^{!})^{!}
, so the homomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\omega,\nu\rangle:=\omega^{KD}\mathbin{\dot{\bullet}}\nu=\big(\omega\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu^{KD}\big)^{KD}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}})\big)\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}

(5.40)

is a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on the internal cohom of the corresponding quadratic algebras. It is given by the matrix
\big((M^{-1})^{\top}\big)^{(1)}N^{(2)}
.

Proposition 5.17
.

If the Hopf algebra
{\mathfrak{R}}
is commutative, then the symmetric monoidal category
\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}})
is coclosed and
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits\big(({\mathcal{B}},\omega),({\mathcal{C}},\nu)\big)
is the pair consisting of the object
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}})
and the corepresentation (
5.40
).

Proof.
Let
\widetilde{\omega}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(\widetilde{\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
be a corepresentation of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
on a quadratic algebra
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
given by a multiplicative matrix
\widetilde{M}
. Due to the definition of internal cohom we have the adjunction

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}}),\widetilde{\mathcal{B}})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{C}},\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{B}})

(5.41)

in the category
{\mathbf{FQA}}
. Let
(w_{i})
and
(w^{i})
be dual bases of the vector spaces
W=({\mathcal{B}}_{1})^{*}
and
W^{*}={\mathcal{B}}_{1}
. Let
(z^{l})
and
(\widetilde{w}^{a})
be bases of
Z^{*}={\mathcal{C}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{W}^{*}=\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
.
The graded homomorphisms
h\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}},{\mathcal{C}})\to\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
and
f\colon{\mathcal{C}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\circ{\mathcal{B}}
related to each other by the bijection (
5.41
) have the form

\displaystyle h\colon w_{i}\otimes z^{k}\mapsto\sum_{a}K^{k}_{ia}\widetilde{w}^{a},

\displaystyle f\colon z^{k}\mapsto\sum_{i,a}K^{k}_{ia}\widetilde{w}^{a}\otimes w^{i}

(5.42)

for the same coefficients
K^{k}_{ia}\in{\mathbb{K}}
. The corepresentation
\langle\omega,\nu\rangle
is given by the matrix
\big((M^{-1})^{\top}\big)^{(1)}N^{(2)}
, hence
h
is a morphism from corepresentation
\langle\omega,\nu\rangle
to the corepresentation
\widetilde{\omega}
iff
\sum_{a}K^{k}_{ia}\widetilde{M}^{a}_{b}=\sum_{j,l}(M^{-1})^{j}_{i}N^{k}_{l}K^{l}_{jb}
.
The condition that the homomorphism
f
is a morphism from
\nu
to
\widetilde{\omega}\circ\omega
has the form
\sum_{i,a}K^{k}_{ia}\widetilde{M}^{a}_{b}M^{i}_{j}=\sum_{l}N^{k}_{l}K^{l}_{jb}
. Due to the equivalence of these conditions the adjunction (
5.41
) gives a natural bijection between the morphisms of the corresponding corepresentations. ∎

5.3
S
-embedding of classical representations

Now we ‘embed’ the classical Representation Theory to the Quantum Representation Theory. Let us begin with representations of finite-dimensional algebra on finite-dimensional vector spaces, i.e. with representations in the closed category
({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)
. We embed them to the category of representations in
({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
via the functor
S^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Then we generalise this embedding for the monoidal category
({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)
, which contains
({\mathbf{AffSch}},\times)
and
({\mathbf{AlgSet}},\times)
as (not full) monoidal subcategories.

5.3.1.

Representations of a finite-dimensional algebra as quantum representations.

According to p.
2.4
the lax monoidal functor
S^{*}\colon({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
induces the contravariant functor
\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*})\colon{\mathbf{FAlg}}\to\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
, where we used
\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)={\mathbf{FAlg}}
,
\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)=\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)^{\mathrm{op}}
(see p.
2.4
, p.
2.4
). Let
{\mathfrak{A}}=(V,\mu_{V},\eta_{V})\in{\mathbf{FAlg}}
. As it is described in p.
2.4
and p.
3.3
the actions and representations of the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}
(objects of
\mathop{\mathbf{Lact}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{A}})=\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}})
) are embedded contravariantly to
\mathop{\mathbf{Lcoact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{O}})=\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})
, where
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*}){\mathfrak{A}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
. We call it
S
-embedding
of finite-dimensional representations of
{\mathfrak{A}}
into the category of quantum representations. Let us describe this embedding in details.

First we need to examine how the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}
is translated to the quantum level. Recall that
S^{*}
is a composition of the contravariant and covariant functors
(-)^{*}
and
S
. Since the duality functor
(-)^{*}\colon({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FVect}}^{\mathrm{op}},\otimes)
is a strong monoidal, the dual vector space
V^{*}\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
is equipped with a structure of coalgebra by the maps
\Delta_{V^{*}}\colon V^{*}\xrightarrow{\mu_{V}^{*}}(V\otimes V)^{*}\cong V^{*}\otimes V^{*}
and
\varepsilon_{V^{*}}=\eta_{V}^{*}\colon V^{*}\to{\mathbb{K}}
.
Further, the colax monoidal functor
S\colon({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
translates the coalgebra
{\mathfrak{A}}^{*}=(V^{*},\Delta_{V^{*}},\varepsilon_{V^{*}})
to the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits(S){\mathfrak{A}}^{*}=(SV^{*},\Delta,\varepsilon)
with the morphisms

\Delta\colon SV^{*}\xrightarrow{S\mu_{V}^{*}}S^{*}(V\otimes V)\cong S(V^{*}\otimes V^{*})\xrightarrow{\phi_{V^{*},V^{*}}}SV^{*}\circ SV^{*}
and
\varepsilon=S\eta_{V}^{*}\colon SV^{*}\to S{\mathbb{K}}={\mathbb{K}}[u]
, where
\phi
is the colax monoidal structure of
S
.

Let
vv^{\prime}=\mu_{V}(v\otimes v^{\prime})
be the multiplication in the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}
and
1_{\mathfrak{A}}=\eta_{V}(1)
be its unity. The quadratic algebras
SV^{*}
and
SV^{*}\otimes SV^{*}\cong S^{*}(V\oplus V)
are identified with the algebras of the polynomials on the spaces
V
and
V\times V=V\oplus V
considered as affine spaces (their linear structure gives the grading). Then the structure of the bialgebra
(SV^{*},\Delta_{SV^{*}},\varepsilon_{SV^{*}})
corresponding to the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}
has the form

\displaystyle\Delta_{SV^{*}}\colon SV^{*}\to SV^{*}\otimes SV^{*},

\displaystyle(\Delta_{SV^{*}}p)(v,v^{\prime})=p(vv^{\prime}),

(5.43)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{SV^{*}}\colon SV^{*}\to{\mathbb{K}},

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{SV^{*}}(p)=p(1_{\mathfrak{A}}),

(5.44)

where
p\in SV^{*}
is a polynomial function on
V
and
v,v^{\prime}\in V
. In terms of dual bases
(v_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
and
(v^{i})_{i=1}^{n}
of
V
and
V^{*}
we have

\Delta(v^{k})=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}c_{ij}^{k}v^{i}\otimes v^{j}
,
\varepsilon(v^{k})=d^{k}

for coefficients
c_{ij}^{k},d^{k}\in{\mathbb{K}}
, determined from the formulae

v_{i}v_{j}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}c_{ij}^{k}v_{k}
,
1_{\mathfrak{A}}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}d^{k}v_{k}
.
In particular, this implies the condition (
5.3
).

Consider a representation
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
of the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}
on a finite-dimensional vector space
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
. It corresponds to an action
a\colon V\otimes W\to W
. Due to the natural isomorphism
(V\otimes W)^{*}\cong V^{*}\otimes W^{*}
the functor
(-)^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\to{\mathbf{FVect}}^{\mathrm{op}}
gives the coaction
a^{*}\colon W^{*}\to V^{*}\otimes W^{*}
of the coalgebra
{\mathfrak{A}}^{*}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FVect}})
on the vector space
W^{*}\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
.
Then, by applying
S
we get the coaction
\delta\colon SW^{*}\xrightarrow{Sa^{*}}S(V^{*}\otimes W^{*})\xrightarrow{\phi_{V^{*},W^{*}}}SV^{*}\circ SW^{*}
of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}
on the quadratic algebra
SW^{*}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
.

Thus, the lax monoidal functor
S^{*}
induces the functor
\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}})\to\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{FQA}}}({\mathbb{O}})
, which maps
(W,\rho)
to
(SW^{*},\omega)
, where
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
is a corepresentation corresponding to the coaction
\delta
. This corepresentation has the form

\displaystyle\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\xrightarrow{\Phi_{W,W}}S\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)\big)^{*}\xrightarrow{S\rho^{*}}{\mathbb{O}},

(5.45)

where
\Phi_{W,Z}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits(SW^{*},SZ^{*})\to S\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W,Z)\big)^{*}
is the graded homomorphism whose first order component is the isomorphism
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W^{*},Z^{*})\cong W\otimes Z^{*}\cong(W^{*}\otimes Z)^{*}\cong\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W,Z)^{*}
natural in
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
,
Z\in{\mathbf{Vect}}
.
Thus, the
S
-embedding of representations is the functor

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}})\to\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle(W,\rho)\mapsto(TW^{*},\omega).

(5.46)

Let
(w_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
and
(w^{i})_{i=1}^{m}
be dual bases of the vector spaces
W
and
W^{*}
respectively. The latter basis gives the isomorphism
SW^{*}={\mathfrak{X}}_{A_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})
. Any representation
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
has the form
\rho(v)w_{j}=\rho^{i}_{j}(v)w_{i}
where
\rho^{i}_{j}\colon V\to{\mathbb{K}}
are linear functions such that

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{j}(vv^{\prime})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\rho^{i}_{k}(v)\rho^{k}_{j}(v^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\rho^{i}_{j}(1_{\mathfrak{A}})=\delta^{i}_{j}.

(5.47)

These functions form an
m\times m
matrix
M
over
SV^{*}
with the entries
M^{i}_{j}=\rho^{i}_{j}\in V^{*}=(SV^{*})_{1}
. Due to the formulae (
5.43
), (
5.44
) the conditions (
5.47
) means exactly that
M
is a multiplicative matrix. Since
SV^{*}
is a commutative algebra this is a usual Manin matrix. Thus
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
is the multiplicative first order
A_{m}
-Manin matrix defining the corepresentation (
5.45
). Then, by using Prop.
5.4
one can show that the functor (
5.46
) is fully faithful.

Remark 5.18
.

The actions
a\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\otimes W\to W
of an infinite-dimensional algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}=(V,\mu_{V},\eta_{V})
on a vector space
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
can be also translated to the graded homomorphisms
\delta\colon SW^{*}\to S(V^{*}\otimes W^{*})\to SV^{*}\circ SW^{*}
due to the isomorphism
(V\otimes W)^{*}\cong V^{*}\otimes W^{*}
natural in
V\in{\mathbf{Vect}}
and
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
. However, the multiplication
\mu_{V}\colon V\otimes V\to V
does not give a comultiplication
V^{*}\to V^{*}\otimes V^{*}
in general, since the natural embedding
V^{*}\otimes W^{*}\hookrightarrow(V\otimes W)^{*}
is not bijective for some infinite-dimensional vector spaces, even if
V=W
. To consider representations of this algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}
as quantum representations we need to introduce a topology on
{\mathfrak{A}}
and to replace
{\mathbf{Vect}}
with an appropriate category of topological
{\mathbb{K}}
-modules
W
. This could extend Quantum Representation Theory for infinitely generated quadratic algebras and allow to consider the classical (including infinite-dimensional) representations of an arbitrary algebra at the quantum level.

Remark 5.19
.

The dequantisation functor
(-)_{1}^{*}\colon{\mathbf{QA}}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{Vect}}
with the strong monoidal structure
({\mathbf{QA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)\to({\mathbf{Vect}},\otimes)
translates a corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathbb{O}}

of a comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=({\mathcal{A}},\Delta,\varepsilon)
on
{\mathcal{B}}={\mathfrak{X}}_{A}({\mathbb{K}})\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
to its “classical limit” – the representation
\rho=\omega_{1}^{*}\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
of the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}=({\mathcal{A}}_{1}^{*},\Delta_{1}^{*},\varepsilon_{1}^{*})
on the vector space
W={\mathcal{B}}_{1}^{*}
.
The entries of the operators
\rho(v)\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
have the form
\rho^{i}_{j}(v)=v(M^{i}_{j})
, where
M=(M^{i}_{j})
is the multiplicative first order
B
-Manin matrix over
{\mathcal{A}}
defining the corepresentation
\omega
. In particular, this functor recovers a classical finite-dimensional representation of a finite-dimensional algebra after its lifting to the quantum level by the
S
-embedding (
5.46
). For a finite-dimensional representation
\rho
of an infinite-dimensional algebra there can also exist a quantum representation with the classical limit
\rho
, but it is not guaranteed.

5.3.2.

Representations of a semi-linear affine monoid scheme as quantum representations.

In p.
4.3
we extended the functor
S^{*}
to all the semi-linear affine schemes. It has a structure of a lax monoidal functor
S^{*}\colon({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
, so it translates actions and representations of a monoid
{\mathbb{S}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)
to actions and representations of the corresponding comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*}){\mathbb{S}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c},\circ)
.

Recall that any semi-linear affine monoid scheme
{\mathbb{S}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)
is given by a bialgebra
({\mathfrak{R}},\alpha,\beta)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{CommAlg}},\otimes)
and a vector space
V\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
with linear maps
t\colon V^{*}\to{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\otimes V^{*}
,
\varepsilon_{V^{*}}\colon V^{*}\to{\mathbb{K}}
such that the diagrams (
4.3
) commute (see p.
4.3
). Hence the monoid
{\mathbb{S}}
can be regarded as the coalgebra
{\mathfrak{C}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*},\Delta_{\mathfrak{C}},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{C}})
with a comultiplication
\Delta_{\mathfrak{C}}=\sigma^{(23)}\cdot(\alpha,t)\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\to{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}
and a counit
\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{C}}=\beta\otimes\varepsilon_{V^{*}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\to{\mathbb{K}}
. The comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*}){\mathbb{S}}
is the semi-connected quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathfrak{R}}\otimes SV^{*}
with morphisms
\Delta\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathcal{A}}
and
\varepsilon\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathbb{K}}[u]
whose first order components are
\Delta_{\mathfrak{C}}
and
\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{C}}
.

Let us write more detailed formulae for the case
{\mathbb{S}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{SLAlgSet}},\otimes)
, i.e. for a semi-linear algebraic monoid. The role of the affine scheme
\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits{\mathfrak{R}}
is played by an algebraic set
X
with the algebra of functions
A(X)={\mathfrak{R}}
. It is equipped with a structure of algebraic monoid
(X,\mu_{X},\eta_{X})
such that
\alpha=\mu_{X}^{*}
and
\beta=\eta_{X}^{*}
; the maps
t
and
\varepsilon_{V^{*}}
are given by
f\colon X\times X\times(V\otimes V)\to V
and
1_{V}\in V
(see p.
4.3
).
Let
(w_{i})
and
(w^{i})
be dual bases of vector spaces
W
and
W^{*}
.
A representation
\rho\colon X\times V\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
and the corresponding action
a\colon X\times(V\otimes W)\to W
have the form
\rho(x,v)w_{j}=a(x,v\otimes w_{j})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\rho^{i}_{j}(x,v)w_{i}
, where
\rho^{i}_{j}\in A(X)\otimes V^{*}
are semi-linear functions on
X\times V
.
The commutativity of the diagrams (
2.4
) is equivalent to
\rho^{i}_{j}\big(xy,f_{x,y}(v\otimes v^{\prime})\big)=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\rho^{i}_{k}(x,v)\rho^{k}_{j}(y,v^{\prime})
and
\rho^{i}_{j}(e,1_{V})=\delta^{i}_{j}
. This means, in turn, that the matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
is multiplicative:
\Delta(\rho^{i}_{j})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}\rho^{i}_{k}\otimes\rho^{k}_{j}
,
\varepsilon(\rho^{i}_{j})=\delta^{i}_{j}
.
In particular, for
X=\{0\}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits{\mathbb{K}}
we have the case of p.
5.3
with
\rho^{i}_{j}(v)=\rho^{i}_{j}(0,v)
.

For general
{\mathfrak{R}}\in{\mathbf{CommAlg}}
denote
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits{\mathfrak{R}}
. An action
a\colon X\times(V\otimes W)\to W
is given by a linear map
a^{*}\colon W^{*}\to{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\otimes W^{*}
that should be a coaction of the coalgebra
{\mathfrak{C}}
on the space
W^{*}
. It has the form
a^{*}(w^{i})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\rho^{i}_{j}\otimes w^{j}
for some
\rho^{i}_{j}\in{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}
such that
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
is a multiplicative matrix. The corresponding representation
\rho=\theta^{-1}(a)\colon X\times V\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
is given by the coalgebra morphism
\rho^{*}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)^{*}\to{\mathfrak{C}}
,
w^{i}\otimes w_{j}\mapsto\rho^{i}_{j}
.
The lax monoidal functor
S^{*}\colon({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
translates the representation
\rho
to the corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
. It also has the form (
5.45
). In terms of bases it is given by the
A_{m}
-Manin matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
. We obtain an extension of the
S
-embedding (
5.46
) for the representations of
{\mathbb{S}}
. This is the fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{S}})\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle(W,\rho)\mapsto(SW^{*},\omega),

(5.48)

where
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*})({\mathbb{S}})
.

5.3.3.

Representations of an algebraic monoid or group as quantum representations.

Consider an algebraic monoid
{\mathbb{M}}=(X,\mu_{X},\eta_{X})
, this is an algebraic set
X
equipped with the multiplication
\mu_{X}\colon X\times X\to X
and the unity
\eta_{X}\colon\{0\}\to X
which give a structure of monoid in
{\mathbf{AlgSet}}
. We use the notations
xy=\mu_{X}(x,y)
,
e=\eta_{X}(0)
. The (not full) embedding
{\mathbf{AlgSet}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{SLAlgSet}}
gives a semi-linear algebraic monoid
{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}:=(X\times{\mathbb{K}},\mu_{X}\times\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{K}},\eta_{X}\times\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{K}})
, so we can apply the results of p.
5.3
. The function algebra
{\mathfrak{R}}=A(X)
is a commutative bialgebra with the comultiplication
(\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}f)(x,y)=f(xy)
and counit
\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}(f)=f(e)
. Let
\rho\colon{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
be a representation of the algebraic monoid
{\mathbb{M}}
on
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
. In the basis
(w_{i})
it has the form
\rho(x)w_{j}=\sum_{i}\rho^{i}_{j}(x)w_{i}
, where
\rho^{i}_{j}\in A(X)
. The corresponding action
(x,w)\mapsto x.w=\rho(x)w
satisfies
(xy).w=x.(y.w)
and
e.w=w
, which is equivalent to the condition of multiplicativity of the matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
is multiplicative. This matrix gives the corresponding corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
, where
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*}){\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\Delta,\varepsilon)
is defined by (
5.1
).

If
{\mathbb{M}}
is an algebraic group (a group in
{\mathbf{AlgSet}}
), then
{\mathfrak{R}}=A(X)
is a commutative Hopf algebra with the antipode
\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}
,
(\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}f)(x)=f(x^{-1})
, hence the matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
is invertible. The inverse matrix
M^{-1}
has entries
\zeta_{\mathfrak{R}}(\rho^{i}_{j})
. In this case we have the dual representation
\rho^{D}\colon{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W^{*})
. It is defined by the formula
\rho^{D}(x)\xi=\xi_{*}\big(\rho(x^{-1})\big)
. In the dual basis
(w^{i})
it reads
\rho^{D}(x)w^{i}=\sum_{j}\rho^{i}_{j}(x^{-1})w^{j}
, so it is given by the matrix
(M^{-1})^{\top}
. At the quantum level this matrix gives the Koszul dual corepresentation
\omega^{KD}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(\Lambda W)\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
, which we defined in p.
5.2
, since
(SW^{*})^{!}=\Lambda W
. Note that it does not coincide with the quantum representation corresponding to the classical representation
\rho^{D}
, because this quantum representation is a corepresentation on
SW
, not on
\Lambda W
, despite they are defined by the same matrix
(M^{-1})^{\top}
(see the warning in p.
5.1
).

Finite monoids and groups are particular cases of algebraic monoids and groups respectively. We can embed a finite monoid
X\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Set}},\times)
into
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
as a set of isolated points given by the equation
\prod_{i=1}^{N}(x-a_{i})=0
, where
N
is the number of elements in
X
and
a_{1},\ldots,a_{N}
are arbitrary pair-wise different elements of the infinite field
{\mathbb{K}}
. Thus, the description given for general algebraic monoids and groups is valid for the finite case.

5.3.4.

Translation of binary operations with representations by the
S
-embedding.
In the classical representation theory there are two important binary operations with representations: direct sum and tensor product. Consider the question: how the
S
-embedding translates them to the quantum level?

Since the functor
S^{*}
has a strong monoidal structure (
4.27
), it translates direct sum of classical representations on the vector spaces
W,Z\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
to a corepresentation on the quadratic algebra
S^{*}(W\oplus Z)=S(W^{*}\oplus Z^{*})=SW^{*}\otimes SZ^{*}
. The corresponding quantum representations are corepresentations on
{\mathcal{B}}=SW^{*}
and
{\mathcal{C}}=SZ^{*}
, their direct sum defined in p.
5.2
is a corepresentation on the same quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{B}}\otimes{\mathcal{C}}=SW^{*}\otimes SZ^{*}
. Let us describe them in details. Consider a semi-linear monoid
{\mathbb{S}}
as in p.
5.3
. Let
\rho\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
and
\pi\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(Z)
be its representations.
In terms of dual bases
(w_{i})
,
(w^{i})
of
W
,
W^{*}
and
(z_{k})
,
(z^{k})
of
Z
,
Z^{*}
these representations are given by the elements
\rho^{i}_{j}=\rho^{*}(w^{i}\otimes w_{j})\in{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}
and
\pi^{k}_{l}=\pi^{*}(z^{k}\otimes z_{l})\in{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}
. The corresponding corepresentations
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
and
\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*}){\mathbb{S}}
are defined by the multiplicative matrices
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
and
N=(\pi^{k}_{l})
. The direct sum
\rho\oplus\pi\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\oplus Z)
is described via the multiplicative matrix
L=M\oplus N
as

\displaystyle(\rho\oplus\pi)^{*}\colon

\displaystyle w^{i}\otimes w_{j}\mapsto\rho^{i}_{j},

\displaystyle w^{i}\otimes z_{l}\mapsto 0,

\displaystyle z^{k}\otimes w_{j}\mapsto 0,

\displaystyle z^{k}\otimes z_{l}\mapsto\pi^{k}_{l}

(cf. the definition of
L=M\oplus N
in Prop.
5.9
). Since the entries of
M
and
N
belongs to the commutative algebra
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathfrak{R}}\otimes SV^{*}
, the direct sum of
\omega,\nu
exists. This is a corepresentation
\omega\dotplus\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*}\otimes SZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
given by the multiplicative matrix
L=M\oplus N
. On the other hand, the
S
-embedding of the direct sum
\rho\otimes\pi\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\oplus Z)
is a corepresentation
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*}\otimes SZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
defined by the same matrix
L
, and hence it coincides with the direct sum
\omega\dotplus\nu
. Thus, the
S
-embedding translates a direct sum of classical representations to the direct sum of the corresponding quantum representations.
Since the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
is commutative, we have the symmetric monoidal category
(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}}),\dotplus)
and we obtain the following statement.

Proposition 5.20
.

The
S
-embedding (
5.48
) has a structure of symmetric strict monoidal functor
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{S}}),\oplus\big)\to\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},\dotplus\big)
.

The tensor product of the representations
\rho
and
\pi
is defined if
{\mathbb{S}}=(X\times V,\mu_{X\times V},\eta_{X\times V})
is equipped with a structure of a bimonoid
(X\times V,\mu_{X\times V},\eta_{X\times V},\Delta_{X\times V},\varepsilon_{X\times V})
in
({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)
. The comultiplication
\Delta_{X\times V}
and the counit
\varepsilon_{X\times V}
give the monoidal product and unit object in
\mathop{\mathbf{Lact}}\nolimits({\mathbb{S}})
as it is described in p.
2.4
. In order to translate the bimonoid structure to the quantum level we need a strong monoidal embedding functor, but unfortunately the monoidal structure (
4.28
) of the functor
S^{*}
is only lax monoidal, so we can not translate the comultiplication
\Delta_{X\times V}
to the quantum level in general.

Suppose that the monoid
{\mathbb{S}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)
is obtained by the category embedding
{\mathbf{AffSch}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{SLAffSch}}
from an affine monoid scheme
{\mathbb{M}}=(X,\mu_{X},\eta_{X})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{AffSch}},\times)
, where the morphisms
\mu_{X}
and
\eta_{X}
are given by algebra homomorphisms
\alpha\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}
and
\beta\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\to{\mathbb{K}}
, so that
{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}=(X\times{\mathbb{K}},\mu_{X}\times\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{K}},\eta_{X}\times\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{\mathbb{K}})
. Note that an action
X\times({\mathbb{K}}\otimes W)\to W
of
{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}
on
W
is the same as an action
X\times W\to W
of
{\mathbb{M}}
on
W
, hence we can identify the representations of
{\mathbb{M}}
on
W
with the representations of
{\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}
of
W
. In particular, representations
\rho\colon{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
and
\pi\colon{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(Z)
are considered as representations of
{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}
. According to p.
2.5
the monoid
{\mathbb{M}}=(X,\mu_{X},\eta_{X})
has the unique structure of bimonoid
(X,\mu_{X},\eta_{X},\Delta_{X},\varepsilon_{X})
, where the diagonal morphism
\Delta_{X}\colon X\to X\times X
and the unique morphism
\varepsilon_{X}\colon X\to\{0\}
correspond to the multiplication
\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}
and the unity
\eta_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathbb{K}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}
. In particular,
\Delta_{X}
defines the tensor product
\rho\otimes\pi\colon{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\otimes Z)
. In the case
X\in{\mathbf{AlgSet}}
the diagonal morphism has the form
\Delta_{X}(x)=(x,x)
, so we obtain
(\rho\otimes\pi)(x)=\rho(x)\otimes\pi(x)
. For general
X\in{\mathbf{AffSch}}
the representation
\rho\otimes\pi
is a morphism corresponding to the homomorphism
(\rho\otimes\pi)^{*}\colon S^{*}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(W\otimes Z)\big)\to{\mathfrak{R}}
,
\widehat{w}\otimes\widehat{z}\mapsto\rho^{*}(\widehat{w})\pi^{*}(\widehat{z})
, where
\widehat{w}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)^{*}
,
\widehat{z}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(Z)^{*}
,
\widehat{w}\otimes\widehat{z}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)^{*}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(Z)^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\otimes Z)^{*}\subset S^{*}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(W\otimes Z)\big)
. In terms of bases one yields
(\rho\otimes\pi)^{*}(w^{i}\otimes w_{j}\otimes z^{k}\otimes z_{l})=\rho^{i}_{j}\pi^{k}_{l}
.

The
\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits
-functor of the embedding
{\mathbf{Alg}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}
,
{\mathfrak{R}}\mapsto{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u]
, translates the bimonoid
({\mathfrak{R}},\mu_{\mathfrak{R}},\eta_{\mathfrak{R}},\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}=\alpha,\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}=\beta)
to the bimonoid
{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\circ{\mathbb{K}}[u],\mu,\eta,\Delta,\varepsilon)
defined by the formulae (
5.1
), (
5.32
). The commutative multiplication
\mu=\mu_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}[u]}
gives the tensor product of corepresentations, which always exists in this case. Thereby, we get the tensor product
\omega\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*}\circ SZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
. It corresponds to the multiplicative usual Manin matrix
P=M\mathbin{\dot{\otimes}}N=M^{(1)}N^{(2)}
with the entries
P^{ik}_{jl}=\rho^{i}_{j}\pi^{k}_{l}
. On the other hand,
\rho\otimes\pi
is translated to the corepresentation
\lambda\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big(S(W^{*}\otimes Z^{*})\big)\to{\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
given by the same matrix
P=M\mathbin{\dot{\otimes}}N
. Due to Corollary
5.5
this means that the graded homomorphism
\phi_{W,Z}\colon S(W^{*}\otimes Z^{*})\to SW^{*}\circ SZ^{*}
is a morphism between the corepresentations
\lambda
and
\omega\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu
. We can formulate it in the following form.

Proposition 5.21
.

In the case
{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}
the
S
-embedding (
5.48
) has a structure of symmetric lax monoidal functor
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{S}}_{\mathbb{M}}),\otimes\big)\to\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{B}}_{\mathfrak{R}})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\big)
.

Remark 5.22
.

The
S
-embedding of
\rho\oplus\pi
differs from the coproduct of
\omega
and
\nu
in general, since
S^{*}(W\oplus Z)\neq S^{*}(W)\amalg S^{*}(Z)
.

5.3.5.

Dualisation of a finite-dimensional bialgebra.

For representations of a finite-dimensional algebra equipped with a bialgebra structure there is another way to translate them to the quantum level such that the representation spaces are translated via the same functor
S^{*}
.

Let
{\mathfrak{B}}=(V,\mu_{V},\eta_{V},\Delta_{V},\varepsilon_{V})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)
