be a finite-dimensional bialgebra. This is an algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}=(V,\mu_{V},\eta_{V})\in{\mathbf{FAlg}}
with a comultiplication
\Delta_{\mathfrak{A}}=\Delta_{V}\colon V\to V\otimes V
and a counit
\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{A}}=\varepsilon_{V}\colon V\to{\mathbb{K}}
. Application of the contravariant strong monoidal functor
(-)^{*}\colon({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)
gives the dual bialgebra

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}=\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits\big((-)^{*}\big){\mathfrak{B}}=(V^{*},\mu_{V^{*}}=\Delta_{V}^{*},\eta_{V^{*}}=\varepsilon_{V}^{*},\Delta_{V^{*}}=\mu_{V}^{*},\varepsilon_{V^{*}}=\eta_{V}^{*})

(5.49)

(see e.g.
[
Kass
, § 3.2, Ex. 1]
). Consider the latter one as a comonoid in
({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
. The embedding
{\mathbf{Alg}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}
gives the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}=({\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\Delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}}_{\mathrm{s}c},\circ)
defined by (
5.1
). It differs from the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(S^{*}){\mathfrak{A}}=(SV^{*},\Delta_{SV^{*}},\varepsilon_{SV^{*}})
used in p.
5.3
, but sometimes we can translate a representation of
{\mathfrak{A}}
to a corepresentations of
{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
.

Suppose first that the bialgebra
{\mathfrak{B}}
is cocommutative. Then the dual bialgebra (
5.49
) is commutative, so by virtue of Prop.
2.2
the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
has the structure of commutative bimonoid
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}=({\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u],\mu,\eta,\Delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}}_{\mathrm{s}c},\circ)
defined by (
5.32
). In this case any representation
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
on a vector space
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
is translated to a corepresentations of
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
on
SW^{*}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}
. Indeed, due to the commutativity of
\mu_{V^{*}}
the linear map
(SV^{*})_{1}=V^{*}\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\sim$}}}({\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}})_{1}
induces the graded homomorphism
SV^{*}\to{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[u]
. In this way we obtain a comonoid morphism
{\mathbb{O}}\to{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
. By composing it with (
5.45
) we get the corepresentation
\omega^{\prime}
of the bimonoid
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
on the quadratic algebra
SW^{*}
:

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\xrightarrow{\Phi_{W,W}}S\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)\big)^{*}\xrightarrow{S\rho^{*}}{\mathbb{O}}\to{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}.

(5.50)

Let
(w_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
be a basis of
W
and
\rho^{i}_{j}\in V^{*}
be such that
\rho(v)w_{j}=\sum_{i}\rho^{i}_{j}(v)w_{i}
, then
\rho
is translated to the corepresentation
\omega^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
given by the usual Manin matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
. In this way we obtain the fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}})\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}})^{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle(W,\rho)\mapsto(SW^{*},\omega^{\prime}).

(5.51)

Let
\pi\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(Z)
be a representation of
{\mathfrak{A}}
on a vector space
Z\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
. The functor (
5.51
) translates
\pi
to a corepresentation
\nu^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
. The direct sum
\rho\oplus\pi\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\oplus Z)
is translated to
\omega^{\prime}\dotplus\nu^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*}\otimes SZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
, so we can regard (
5.51
) as a strict monoidal functor
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}}),\oplus\big)\to\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}})^{\mathrm{op}},\dotplus\big)
.
The translation of the tensor product by the functor (
5.51
) gives a lax monoidal functor
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}}),\otimes\big)\to\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\big)
. If
{\mathfrak{B}}
is a Hopf algebra with an antipode
\zeta_{V}\colon V\to V
, then (
5.49
) is also a Hopf algebra, its antipode is
\zeta_{V}^{*}\colon V^{*}\to V^{*}
(see
[
Kass
, § 3.3, Prop. 3.3.3]
), so
{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
is a Hopf monoid and, hence, one can define the Koszul dual corepresentation
(\omega^{\prime})^{KD}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(\Lambda W)\to{\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
.

Let
{\mathbb{M}}=(X,\mu_{X},\eta_{X})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Set}},\times)
be a finite monoid. The algebra
{\mathfrak{B}}={\mathbb{K}}[{\mathbb{M}}]
defined in p.
3.2
is a cocommutative bialgebra with the coalgebra structure

\displaystyle\Delta_{V}\Big(\sum_{x\in X}\alpha_{x}x\Big)=\sum_{x\in X}\alpha_{x}(x\otimes x),

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{V}\Big(\sum_{x\in X}\alpha_{x}x\Big)=\sum_{x\in X}\alpha_{x}.

(5.52)

It is a Hopf algebra iff
{\mathbb{M}}
is a group. The dual bialgebra
{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}
coincides with the bialgebra
{\mathfrak{R}}=A(X)
constructed in p.
5.3
. A representation
{\mathbb{M}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
is uniquely extended to a representation
{\mathbb{K}}[{\mathbb{M}}]\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
. If we translate these representations to the corepresentations
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{R}}}
and
\omega^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
respectively, then the results coincide:
\omega=\omega^{\prime}
, so in this case the functor (
5.51
) is just another description of the representation
S
-embedding obtained in p.
5.3
.

For a non-cocommutative bialgebra
{\mathfrak{B}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Bimon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)
we can not translate an arbitrary representation
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
to a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
, but if the corresponding matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
is an
A_{m}
-Manin matrix as a matrix over
{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}
, then we have a corepresentation
\omega^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(SW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
given by this matrix, since this matrix is multiplicative with respect to
\Delta_{V^{*}}
and
\varepsilon_{V^{*}}
anyway.

5.4
T
-embedding of classical representations

An alternative way to translate a classical representation to the quantum level is to use the functor
T^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\to{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
defined in p.
4.1
instead of
S^{*}
.

5.4.1.

T
-embedding of a finite-dimensional algebra and its representations.

Let
{\mathfrak{A}}=(V,\mu_{V},\eta_{V})\in{\mathbf{FAlg}}
. The strong monoidal functor
T^{*}\colon({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
induces the functor
\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(T^{*})
, which maps the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)
to the comonoid

{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(T^{*}){\mathfrak{A}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits(T^{\mathrm{op}})(V^{*},\mu_{V}^{*},\eta_{V}^{*})=(TV^{*},\Delta,\varepsilon)
in the category
({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ),

where
\Delta\colon TV^{*}\xrightarrow{T\mu_{V}^{*}}T^{*}(V\otimes V)\cong T(V^{*}\otimes V^{*})\cong TV^{*}\circ TV^{*}
,
\varepsilon=T\eta_{V}^{*}\colon TV^{*}\to T{\mathbb{K}}={\mathbb{K}}[u]
.

Due to Prop.
4.5
we obtain
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits(TW^{*},TZ^{*})=T\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W^{*},Z^{*})\big)\cong T\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W,Z)\big)^{*}
, so the morphisms (
3.48
) for the monoidal functor
F=T^{*}\colon({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
is the isomorphisms
\Phi_{W,Z}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf cohom}}\nolimits(TW^{*},TZ^{*})\xrightarrow{\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\sim$}}}T\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W,Z)\big)^{*}
given by the same identification
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W^{*},Z^{*})\cong\mathop{\mathrm{\bf hom}}\nolimits(W,Z)^{*}
as for
S^{*}
.

Let
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
be a representation of the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}
on
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
. In a basis
(w_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
of
W
it is given by the linear functions
\rho^{i}_{j}\in V^{*}
satisfying (
5.47
). By using the isomorphism
\Phi_{W,W}
we obtain the corresponding quantum representation

\displaystyle\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TW^{*})\cong T\big(\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)\big)^{*}\xrightarrow{T\rho^{*}}{\mathbb{O}}.

(5.53)

This is a corepresentation of
{\mathbb{O}}=(TV^{*},\Delta,\varepsilon)
on the quadratic algebra
TW^{*}={\mathfrak{X}}_{0_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})
, where
0_{m}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
is the zero idempotent and the identification
W={\mathbb{K}}^{m}
is fixed by the basis
(w_{i})
. Recall that any
m\times m
matrix is a
0_{m}
-Manin matrix. The corepresentation (
5.53
) is defined by the multiplicative first order matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
.
Thus, the
T
-embedding
of the representations is the fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}})\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle(W,\rho)\mapsto(TW^{*},\omega).

(5.54)

5.4.2.

T
-embedding of a semi-linear affine monoid scheme and its representations.

The functor
T^{*}
can be extended to the functor
T^{*}\colon{\mathbf{SLAffSch}}\to{\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}}
by the formula
T^{*}\colon(\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\nolimits{\mathfrak{R}})\times V\mapsto{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes TV^{*}
with the obvious mapping of the morphisms. This is a strong monoidal functor
({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
.

Let
{\mathbb{S}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)
be a semi-linear affine monoid scheme as in p.
5.3
. Then the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(T^{*}){\mathbb{S}}
is the semi-connected quadratic algebra
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathfrak{R}}\otimes T^{*}V
with the graded homomorphisms
\Delta\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathcal{A}}\circ{\mathcal{A}}
and
\varepsilon\colon{\mathcal{A}}\to{\mathbb{K}}[u]
such that their first order components
\Delta_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{1}
coincide with
\Delta_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\to{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\otimes{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}
and
\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{C}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}\to{\mathbb{K}}
, which were described in p.
5.3
.

Let
\rho\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
be a representation of
{\mathbb{S}}
on a space
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
with a basis
(w_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
. It is given by the coalgebra morphism
\rho^{*}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)^{*}\to{\mathfrak{C}}
,
w^{i}\otimes w_{j}\to\rho^{i}_{j}
, where
{\mathfrak{C}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*},\Delta_{\mathfrak{C}},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{C}})
and
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
is a multiplicative first order matrix over the algebra
{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes TV^{*}
with respect to
\Delta
and
\varepsilon
. Since the functor
T^{*}\colon({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
is strong monoidal, it translates
\rho
to the corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
corresponding to the
0_{m}
-Manin matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
.
This extends
T
-embedding functor (
5.54
) to the fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{S}})\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle(W,\rho)\mapsto(TW^{*},\omega),

(5.55)

where
{\mathbb{O}}=\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits(T^{*}){\mathbb{S}}
.

5.4.3.

Translation of binary operations under the
T
-embedding.

Let representations
\rho\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
,
\pi\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(Z)
be given by the elements
\rho^{i}_{j},\pi^{k}_{l}\in{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes V^{*}
in bases
(w_{i})
and
(z_{k})
. Denote the corresponding corepresentations
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
given by the matrices
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
and
N=(\pi^{k}_{l})
by
\omega
and
\nu
.
The functor
T^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\to{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
has a structure of strong monoidal functor (
4.41
). By using Corollary
5.5
again we see that the
T
-embedding translates the direct sum representation
\rho\oplus\pi\colon{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\oplus Z)
to the coproduct
\omega\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\nu\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TW^{*}\amalg TZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}
given by the matrix
L=M\oplus N
.
This means that
T
-embedding (
5.55
) preserves the finite products. In other words, the functor (
5.55
) has a structure of symmetric strong monoidal functor
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{S}}),\oplus\big)\to\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\big)
.

By taking into account the lax monoidal structure (
4.40
) of the category embedding
T^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
we see that the
T
-embedding gives a symmetric lax monoidal functor
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{S}}),\oplus\big)\to\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},\dotplus\big)
.

Since
T^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
has a structure of symmetric strong monoidal functor (
4.42
), we can apply the results of p.
3.3
. In this way we obtain a strong monoidal functor
\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathbb{S}}),\otimes\big)\to\big(\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FQA}}({\mathbb{O}})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\big)
given by the
T
-embedding (
5.55
).

5.4.4.

Representations of a finite-dimensional bialgebra.

Let
{\mathfrak{B}}=(V,\mu_{V},\eta_{V},\Delta_{V},\varepsilon_{V})
be a bialgebra as in p.
5.3
. Then we can translate any representation of the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}=(V,\mu_{V},\eta_{V})
on
W\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
to a quantum representation of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
on
TW^{*}
. We obtain the fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbf{Rep}}\nolimits_{\mathbf{FVect}}({\mathfrak{A}})\hookrightarrow\mathop{\mathbf{Corep}}\nolimits_{{\mathbf{FQA}}}({\mathbb{B}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}})^{\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle(W,\rho)\mapsto(TW^{*},\omega^{\prime}),

(5.56)

where
\rho\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W)
,
\rho(v)w_{j}=\sum_{i}\rho^{i}_{j}(v)w_{i}
,
\rho^{i}_{j}\in V^{*}
and
\omega^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TW^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
is the corepresentation given by the multiplicative matrix
M=(\rho^{i}_{j})
. Note that in this case we do not need to require the condition of cocommutativity of
{\mathfrak{B}}
nor any Manin condition on
M
.

Let
\pi\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(Z)
be representation and
\nu^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
be the corresponding corepresentation. The embedding (
5.56
) translates direct sum
\rho\oplus\pi\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\oplus Z)
to the coproduct
\omega^{\prime}\mathbin{\dot{\sqcup}}\nu^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TW^{*}\amalg TZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
. It translates the tensor product of representations
\rho\otimes\pi\colon{\mathfrak{A}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits(W\otimes Z)
defined by means of
\Delta_{V}
to the tensor product
\omega^{\prime}\mathbin{\dot{\circ}}\nu^{\prime}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits(TW^{*}\circ TZ^{*})\to{\mathbb{O}}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{*}}
.

6
Examples

6.1
Corepresentations of
M_{q}(m)

The relationship of some Manin matrices with the Lax operators of
U_{q}(\mathfrak{gl}_{n})
type was described in details in
[
S
]
. It can be interpreted in terms of Quantum Representation Theory.

6.1.1.

Matrix algebra.

Consider the algebra
{\mathfrak{A}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
. It consists of
m\times m
matrices over
{\mathbb{K}}
. The multiplication in
{\mathfrak{A}}
is the usual matrix multiplication and the unity is the identity matrix. The quadratic algebra
M(m)=M(m,{\mathbb{K}})=S{\mathfrak{A}}^{*}
is the algebra of regular functions on
\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
(as on an algebraic set). It is generated by the functions
a^{i}_{j}\colon M\mapsto M^{i}_{j}
,
M\in\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
. Thus
M(m)
is the graded algebra generated by
m^{2}
order 1 elements
a^{i}_{j}\in{\mathfrak{A}}^{*}
. The multiplication and unity of
\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
gives the structure of comonoid on
M(m)
. We obtain the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}=M(m)=(M(m),\Delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
, where the graded homomorphisms
\Delta\colon M(m)\to M(m)\circ M(m)
,
\varepsilon\colon M(m)\to{\mathbb{K}}
have the form

\displaystyle\Delta(a^{i}_{j})=\sum_{k=1}^{m}a^{i}_{k}\otimes a^{k}_{j},

\displaystyle\varepsilon(a^{i}_{j})=\delta^{i}_{j}.

(6.1)

The algebra
M(m)
with this structure can be considered as a bialgebra, but this is not a Hopf algebra.

The matrices from
\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
act on elements of
W={\mathbb{K}}^{m}
as on column vectors. The corresponding representation
\rho\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m})
is the isomorphism identifying operators on
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
with the matrices. By the
S
-embedding described in p.
5.3
we get the corepresentation
\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)\to M(m)
given by the matrix
M=(a^{i}_{j})
.

The right action of
\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
on the space
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
considered as the space of row-vectors gives the representation
\rho^{\top}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m})
. The corresponding corepresentation
\omega^{\top}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)\to M(m)^{\mathrm{cop}}
is defined by the transposed matrix
M^{\top}
. Note that the commutation relations
a^{i}_{j}a^{k}_{l}=a^{k}_{l}a^{i}_{j}
, which define the quadratic algebra
M(m)
, are equivalent to the requirement that both
M
and
M^{\top}
are
A_{m}
-Manin matrices (see e.g.
[
S
, Prop. 3.1]
).

6.1.2.

A
q
-deformation of
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
.

Consider the
{\mathbb{K}}
-plane
{\mathbb{K}}^{2}
. At the quantum level it is described by the quadratic algebra
{\mathfrak{X}}_{A_{2}}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathbb{K}}[x,y]
. Let
q\in{\mathbb{K}}\backslash\{0\}
. Replacement of the commutation relation
yx=xy
by
yx=qxy
gives a
q
-deformation of
{\mathbb{K}}[x,y]
called
quantum plane
. It can be interpreted as a
q
-deformation of the vector space
{\mathbb{K}}^{2}
in the category of quantum linear spaces (where the vector spaces considered as quadratic algebras via the embedding
S^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
).

For a general dimension
m
a quantum
q
-deformation of
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
is constructed as the quadratic algebra generated by
x^{1},\ldots,x^{m}
with the commutation relations
x^{j}x^{i}=qx^{i}x^{j}
,
i<j
. This is the algebra
{\mathfrak{X}}_{A^{q}_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})
for the idempotent
A_{m}^{q}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
defined by the formulae
4
4

4

More generally, one can construct a multi-parametric deformation
[
Man89
]
by imposing the conditions
q_{ij}=q_{ji}^{-1}
,
q_{ii}=1
only (see
[
S
, § 3.3]
).

\displaystyle A^{q}_{m}=\frac{1}{2}(1-P^{q}_{m}),

\displaystyle(P^{q}_{m})^{ij}_{kl}=q_{ji}\delta^{i}_{l}\delta^{j}_{k},

(6.2)

q_{ij}=q_{ji}^{-1}=q
for
i<j
,
q_{ii}=1
.

6.1.3.

A
q
-deformation of the matrix algebra.

Let
M_{q}(m)=M_{q}(m,{\mathbb{K}})
be the bialgebra generated by
a^{i}_{j}
,
i,j=1,\ldots,m
, with the commutation relations

\displaystyle\begin{split}&a^{j}_{k}a^{i}_{k}=qa^{i}_{k}a^{j}_{k},\qquad\qquad a^{i}_{l}a^{j}_{k}=a^{j}_{k}a^{i}_{l},\\
&a^{i}_{l}a^{i}_{k}=qa^{i}_{k}a^{i}_{l},\qquad\qquad a^{i}_{k}a^{j}_{l}-a^{j}_{l}a^{i}_{k}=(q^{-1}-q)a^{j}_{k}a^{i}_{l},\end{split}

(6.3)

where
i<j
,
k<l
, the comultiplication and counit have the same form (
6.1
) (see e.g.
[
Kass
, § 4.10]
,
[
RTF
]
). Since these commutation relations are quadratic and the maps (
6.1
) satisfy (
5.2
) (for
k=1
) the algebra
M_{q}(m)
can be considered as a comonoid in
({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
.

In
[
Man87
,
Man88
]
Manin interpreted
M_{q}(2)
in terms of the quantum plane and gave a description of related quantum groups via the quantum linear spaces. In the case of the bialgebra
M_{q}(m)
this approach can be formulated as the following fact, which was discussed in the works
[
CFR
, § 3.3, Prop. 2]
,
[
CFRS
, § 2.4, Prop. 2.4]
,
[
S
, § 4.1, Th. 4.4]
.

Proposition 6.1
.

The relations (
6.3
) are equivalent to the requirement that both
M=(a^{i}_{j})
and its transposed
M^{\top}
are
A^{q}_{m}
-Manin matrices.

It follows that the
A^{q}_{m}
-Manin matrices
M=(a^{i}_{j})
and
M^{\top}
over
M_{q}(m)
define the corepresentations

\displaystyle\omega_{q}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A^{q}_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)\to M_{q}(m),

\displaystyle\omega_{q}^{\top}\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A^{q}_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)\to M_{q}(m)^{\mathrm{cop}}

(6.4)

respectively.
These corepresentations are
q
-deformations of the standard matrix representations
\rho
and
\rho^{\top}
defined in p.
6.1
. Namely, we apply the embedding
S^{*}
to the matrix algebra
\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
, to the representation space
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
, to the representations
\rho
,
\rho^{\top}
and then we simultaneously deform them all.

If
m=2
we obtain the quantum representation of
M_{q}(2)
on the quantum plane. This case is described in details in
[
Man87
,
Man88
,
Kass
]
in terms of bialgebras and coactions in the monoidal category
({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
.

The comonoid
M_{q}(m)
and its quantum representations (
6.4
) can be interpreted in the following way. Consider the graded homomorphisms
f\colon T\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})\big)^{*}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A^{q}_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)
and
f^{\top}\colon T\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})\big)^{*}\to\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A^{q}_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)^{\mathrm{cop}}
defined as
f(a^{i}_{j})={\mathcal{M}}^{i}_{j}
and
f^{\top}(a^{i}_{j})={\mathcal{M}}^{j}_{i}
, where
a^{i}_{j}\in\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})\big)^{*}
are linear functions on the vector space
\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})
(see p.
6.1
) and
{\mathcal{M}}^{i}_{j}
are entries of the universal
A^{q}_{m}
-Manin matrix. These homomorphisms are morphisms in the category
\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
, since they preserve the comultiplications and counits.
Prop.
6.1
means exactly that the graded algebra
M_{q}(m)=M_{q}(m,{\mathbb{K}})
is the pushout

\textstyle{T\big(\mathop{\mathrm{Mat}}\nolimits_{m}({\mathbb{K}})\big)^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\top}}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A^{q}_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)^{\mathrm{cop}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{q}^{\top}}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A^{q}_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{q}}
\textstyle{M_{q}(m)}

in the category
{\mathbf{GrAlg}}
and, consequently, in
{\mathbf{FQA}}
(see
[
MacLane
, § 3.3]
for a definition of pushout). Moreover, the comonoid
M_{q}(m)
is a pushout of
f
and
f^{\top}
in the category
\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
.

6.2
An extension of the Yangian
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})
and its corepresentations

In
[
CF
]
the authors presented an example of a usual Manin matrix defined as a product of the Lax operator for the
\mathfrak{gl}_{m}
Yangian with a shift operator. We interpret this extension as a corepresentation of some extension of this Yangian on the quadratic algebra
{\mathfrak{X}}_{A_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})
.

6.2.1.

Manin matrix from the
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})
Lax operator.

Denote by
P_{m}
the operator acting by permutation of the tensor factors in
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
, that is
P_{m}=\sigma_{{\mathbb{K}}^{m},{\mathbb{K}}^{m}}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})
. It has entries
(P_{m})^{ij}_{kl}=\delta^{i}_{l}\delta^{j}_{k}
and is related with the anti-symmetrizer as
A_{m}=\dfrac{1-P_{m}}{2}
.

Consider the rational
R
-matrix
R(z)=z-P_{m}
. The Yangian
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})
is the algebra generated (over
{\mathbb{K}}
) by
t_{ij}^{r}
,
i,j=1,\ldots,m
,
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 1}
, with the relations

\displaystyle R(z-v)T^{(1)}(z)T^{(2)}(w)=T^{(2)}(w)T^{(1)}(z)R(z-w),

(6.9)

where
T(z)
is the
m\times m
matrix over
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[[z^{-1}]]
with the entries
T(z)^{i}_{j}=\delta^{i}_{j}+\sum_{r\geqslant 1}t_{ij}^{r}z^{-r}
. The Yangian is equipped with a structure of Hopf algebra:

\displaystyle\Delta\big(T(z)^{i}_{j}\big)=\sum_{k=1}^{m}T(z)^{i}_{k}\otimes T(z)^{k}_{j},

\displaystyle\varepsilon\big(T(z)^{i}_{j}\big)=\delta^{i}_{j},

\displaystyle\zeta\big(T(z)\big)=T(z)^{-1}.

(6.10)

Consider the shift operator
e^{-\frac{\partial}{\partial z}}
. The relation (
6.9
) implies that the matrix
T(z)e^{-\frac{\partial}{\partial z}}
is an
A_{m}
-Manin matrix over the algebra
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[[z^{-1}]][e^{-\frac{\partial}{\partial z}}]
(see details in
[
CF
]
,
[
S
]
). This fact is equivalent to the relation

\displaystyle A_{m}T(z)T(z-1)(1-A_{m})=0.

(6.11)

6.2.2.

Extended Yangian and its corepresentation.

Let us add one more generator
\tau
to the Yangian and postulate the commutation relation

\displaystyle\tau T(z)=T(z-1)\tau,

(6.12)

see
[
Kh
]
; via generators it can be written in the form
\tau t_{ij}^{r}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}{r-1\choose k-1}t_{ij}^{k}\tau
. We obtain a bialgebra, where the comultiplication and counit of the new generator are defined by the formulae
\Delta(\tau)=\tau\otimes\tau
,
\varepsilon(\tau)=1
. This is an extension of
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})
as a bialgebra, denote it by
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[\tau]
. It has the form of the tensor product
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\tau]
as a vector space, but not as an algebra, since
\tau
does not commute with the generators
t_{ij}^{r}
.

The relations (
6.11
), (
6.12
) imply that the matrix
M=T(z)\tau
is an
A_{m}
-Manin matrix over the algebra
{\mathfrak{R}}=Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[\tau]((z^{-1}))
, where we extend the basic field to the field of formal Laurent series

\displaystyle{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))=\Big\{\sum_{k=-\infty}^{N}\alpha_{k}z^{k}\mid N\in{\mathbb{Z}},\alpha_{k}\in{\mathbb{K}}\Big\}.

(6.13)

The
A_{m}
-Manin matrix
M=T(z)\tau
is multiplicative with respect to the comultiplication
\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\to{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}{\mathfrak{R}}
and counit
\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}\colon{\mathfrak{R}}\to{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))
obtained by the field extension, so it gives a corepresentation

\displaystyle\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits\Big({\mathfrak{X}}_{A_{m}}\big({\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))\big)\Big)\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}

(6.14)

of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=\big({\mathfrak{R}}\circ_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))[u],\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))[u]},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))[u]}\big)
, where the operation
\circ_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}
is defined over the extended field (
6.13
), we have the bialgebra isomorphism
{\mathfrak{R}}\circ_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1})))[u]={\mathfrak{R}}\circ_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\mathbb{K}}[u]
over
{\mathbb{K}}
. The quantum representation
\omega
has the classical representation space
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
(with respect to the
S
-embedding), but the quantum algebra we represent is not classical.

In this construction we consider the elements of
{\mathfrak{R}}=Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[\tau]((z^{-1}))
as elements of order
0
, the graded algebra is
{\mathfrak{R}}[u]
. Alternatively one can set
\deg\tau=1
. Then the bialgebra
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[\tau]((z^{-1}))
itself becomes a comonoid in
({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
, and entries of
M=T(z)\tau
become elements of order
1
. This is an affinely generated quadratic algebra over
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})((z^{-1}))
, but it is not semi-connected.

Remark 6.2
.

The localisation of
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[\tau]
by
\tau^{-1}
gives us a Hopf algebra
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[\tau^{\pm 1}]
with the antipode extended as
\zeta(\tau)=\tau^{-1}
. However, the formula
\deg\tau=1
and its consequence
\deg\tau^{-1}=-1
define a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-grading rather than an
{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
-grading on the algebra
Y(\mathfrak{gl}_{m})[\tau^{\pm 1}]
.

6.3
Corepresentations of an extended
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})

As we see in
[
S
, § 7.1]
, the Lax operator of the Yangians
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
and
Y(\mathfrak{sp}_{m})
also give Manin matrices for some idempotents, so we can construct quantum representations for them by analogy with Subsection
6.2
. For simplicity we consider the orthogonal case only. The symplectic version is completely analogues.

Here we suppose that
\mathop{\mathrm{char}}\nolimits{\mathbb{K}}=0
.

6.3.1.

The algebras
X(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
and
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
.

Consider again the collection of generators
t_{ij}^{r}
, where
i,j=1,\ldots,m
,
r\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 1}
. Let
T(u)
be the
m\times m
matrix defined by the same formula as in p.
6.2
. Denote by
X(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
the algebra with these generators and commutation relations (
6.9
) but for another
R
-matrix
R(z)=R_{\mathfrak{so}_{m}}(z)=1-\dfrac{P_{m}}{z}+\dfrac{Q_{m}}{z-m/2+1}
, where
Q_{m}
is the operator on
{\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m}
with the entries
(Q_{m})^{ij}_{kl}=\delta^{i+j}_{m+1}\delta^{m+1}_{k+l}
. The Yangian
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
can be defined as a quotient of
X(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
by the relations
\sum_{k=1}^{m}T(z)^{i}_{k}T(z+\frac{m}{2}-1)^{j}_{m+1-k}=\delta^{i+j}_{m+1}
. Both
X(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
and
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
are Hopf algebras with the comultiplication, counit and antipode of the same form (
6.10
) (see details in
[
AMR
, § 2, § 3, Cor. 3.2]
,
[
Molev
, § 11.1]
and
[
S
, § 7.1]
).
5
5

5

The algebra
X(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
is usually called ‘extended Yangian’, but we use this term for another algebra.

6.3.2.

An algebra
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})
and a
B_{m}
-Manin matrix.

Consider the idempotent

\displaystyle B_{m}=\frac{1-P_{m}}{2}+\frac{Q_{m}}{m}=A_{m}+\frac{Q_{m}}{m}\in\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits({\mathbb{K}}^{m}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}^{m})

(6.15)

(introduced in
[
S
, § 7.1]
). It gives the commutative quadratic algebra
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})
isomorphic to the quotient of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{A_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})={\mathbb{K}}[x^{1},\ldots,x^{m}]
by the quadratic relations

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}x^{i}x^{m+1-i}=0.

(6.16)

It is proved in
[
S
, § 7.1]
that the matrix
T(z)e^{-\frac{\partial}{\partial z}}
is a
B_{m}
-Manin matrix over the algebra
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})[[z^{-1}]][e^{-\frac{\partial}{\partial z}}]
. This is equivalent to the relation

\displaystyle B_{m}T(z)T(z-1)(1-B_{m})=0.

(6.17)

6.3.3.

A corepresentation on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B_{m}}({\mathbb{K}})
.

Let us extend the Yangian
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})
by a new generator
\tau
and impose the relations (
6.12
). This is a bialgebra over
{\mathbb{K}}
(the comultiplication and counit has the same form as for the
\mathfrak{gl}_{m}
case). It is isomorphic to
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\tau]
as a vector space, so we analogously denote it by
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})[\tau]
. The field extension gives the bialgebra
{\mathfrak{R}}=Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})[\tau]((z^{-1}))
over (
6.13
).

Due to (
6.17
) the matrix
M=T(z)\tau
is a
B_{m}
-Manin matrix over
{\mathfrak{R}}
. It is multiplicative with respect to the
{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))
-linear comultiplication and counit of the bialgebra
{\mathfrak{R}}
, so it defines a corepresentation

\displaystyle\omega\colon\mathop{\mathrm{\bf end}}\nolimits\Big({\mathfrak{X}}_{B_{m}}\big({\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))\big)\Big)\to{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}

(6.18)

of the comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}}\circ{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1})))[u],\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))[u]},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits_{{\mathbb{K}}((z^{-1}))[u]})
.

Remark 6.3
.

Since the relation (
6.17
) follows from (
6.9
) only the matrix
T(z)\tau
can be considered as a
B_{m}
-Manin matrix over the bialgebra
X(\mathfrak{so}_{m})[\tau]((z^{-1}))
. By using the localisation we can regard it as a
B_{m}
-Manin matrix over the Hopf algebra
Y(\mathfrak{so}_{m})[\tau^{\pm 1}]((z^{-1}))
or
X(\mathfrak{so}_{m})[\tau^{\pm 1}]((z^{-1}))
.

Conclusion and further directions

Quantum linear spaces and quantum representations.

The Manin product ‘
\circ
’ allows us to construct Quantum Representation Theory on quantum linear spaces
{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Namely, we applied the general approach described in Section
3
to the symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{C}}=({\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
and its subcategory
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
. By using the category embedding
S^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
we can consider the finite-dimensional vector spaces and as quantum linear spaces. Its extension
S^{*}\colon{\mathbf{SLAffSch}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{GrAlg}}^{\mathrm{op}}
helps us to interpret representations of finite-dimensional algebras and algebraic groups as quantum representations. The binary operations with vector spaces and representations agree with their quantum versions (modulo some natural transformations
\phi
).

Semi-linear spaces.

Introduction of semi-linear spaces gives the unified theory of finite-dimensional representations of finite-dimensional algebras and algebraic groups: one needs to apply the approach of Section
3
to
{\mathbf{C}}=({\mathbf{SLAffSch}},\otimes)
and
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{FVect}}
. We have the same situation at the quantum level, where the role of semi-linear spaces is played by semi-connected quadratic algebras:
{\mathbf{C}}=({\mathbf{QA}}_{\mathrm{s}c}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
,
{\mathbf{P}}={\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
.

Multiplicative Manin matrices.

It is shown that the quantum representations of a quantum monoid on
{\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})
is in one-to-one correspondence with the multiplicative
B
-Manin matrices over the corresponding bialgebra. In the case of more general quantum algebra an additional condition on the Manin matrix is imposed: its entries should belong to the first order graded component.

(A,B)
-Manin matrices.

The multiplicative
B
-Manin matrices correspond to the comonoid morphisms from the graded algebra
\mathop{\mathrm{\bf coend}}\nolimits\big({\mathfrak{X}}_{B}({\mathbb{K}})\big)
equipped with the comultiplication and counit (
4.89
). A natural question arises: how to generalise the theory described here in order to include more general
(A,B)
-Manin matrices and cocomposition (
4.88
)? In this case we probably need to consider a family of Manin matrices compatible with the cocompositions and counits in some way. We plan to construct such a generalisation in future works.

Another subcategories of graded algebras.

The quantum linear spaces
{\mathcal{A}}\in{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
can be generalised by considering more wide subcategory of
{\mathbf{GrAlg}}
. The main condition we should keep is the connectivity:
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}={\mathbb{K}}
. For example, one can take all the connected affinely generated graded algebras – this will allow to connect the (generalised) quantum linear spaces with a quantum analogue of the projective spaces. We hope to investigate this case in the future.

Super-case.

If one considers a vector space
V\in{\mathbf{FVect}}
as a purely odd super-vector space, the embedding
\Lambda^{*}\colon{\mathbf{FVect}}\to{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
should be applied (see p.
4.1
). By combining the even and odd embeddings one can embed an arbitrary finite-dimensional super-vector space
V=V_{\bar{0}}\oplus V_{\bar{1}}
into the category of quadratic super-algebras as
S^{*}(V_{\bar{0}})\otimes\Lambda^{*}(V_{\bar{1}})
. The general approach of Section
3
can be applied to the super-case as well. The super-version of Quantum Representation Theory is also a subject for future publications.

S
-embedding vs
T
-embedding.

Both functors
S^{*}
and
T^{*}
lift the vector spaces and representations to the quantum level. These two ways to embed the vector spaces have advantages and disadvantages with respect to each other. The
S
-embedding has more intuitive interpretation and leads to the usual Manin matrices, which have a lot of applications. It has a strong monoidal structure
({\mathbf{FVect}},\oplus)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\otimes)
, but it is not strong monoidal as a monoidal functor
({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
. The
T
-embedding has a structure of strong monoidal functor
({\mathbf{FVect}},\otimes)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\circ)
. As a monoidal functor
({\mathbf{FVect}},\oplus)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\otimes)
it is not strong monoidal, however it has a strong monoidal structure
({\mathbf{FVect}},\oplus)\to({\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}},\amalg)
, so it is more natural to consider coproduct
\amalg
as an analogue of direct sum of vector spaces for the quantum case, when we use the
T
-embedding (note that
\amalg
is exactly the categorical product in
{\mathbf{FQA}}^{\mathrm{op}}
).

Tensor product.

If we translate a finite-dimensional algebra or an algebraic monoid/group to the quantum level by either functor
S^{*}
or
T^{*}
, then the tensor product of quantum representations of the obtained quantum algebra always exists due to the ‘commutativity’ of the classical algebra. For a more general comonoid
{\mathbb{O}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{GrAlg}},\circ)
this existence is not guaranteed even if
{\mathbb{O}}\in\mathop{\mathbf{Comon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{FQA}},\circ)
or
{\mathbb{O}}={\mathbb{O}}_{\mathfrak{R}}
for a quantum monoid/group
{\mathfrak{R}}=({\mathfrak{R}},\Delta_{\mathfrak{R}},\varepsilon_{\mathfrak{R}})\in\mathop{\mathbf{Mon}}\nolimits({\mathbf{Alg}},\otimes)
. It means that the category of quantum representations loses the tensor product under a quantum deformation. We conjecture that the tensor product can be restored in some important cases by means of deformation of the symmetric structure.

Infinite-dimensional case.

By considering the purely algebraic theory of quantum representations we are restricted by the finite-dimensional case by three reasons. First, the functor
(-)^{*}\colon{\mathbf{Vect}}\to{\mathbf{Vect}}^{\mathrm{op}}
is not full (it is only faithful). Second, it is not lax monoidal with respect to
\otimes
(only colax monoidal). Finally, the monoidal category
({\mathbf{QA}},\circ)
is not coclosed (the proof of Prop.
4.5
essentially uses the finiteness of the basis
(w_{i})
). It seems that a generalisation of the results given here to the infinite-dimensional case is possible, if we introduce some kind of topology on vector spaces and algebras.
